### V3 sessions, plans, sync, and realtime

- **Sidechat mutation borrowing boundary:** `store/pebble/session_sidechat_worktree.go:validateSidechatWorktreeBinding` is the non-owning branch of `prepareWorktreeOwnership` for Plan/AI snapshot creation and rebind. It checks the live same-principal parent, exact inherited repository fields, non-nested lineage, retained program claims and any existing ownership/reservation; it emits no ownership record. Runtime Git identity remains a separate required check. Missing legacy ownership projections are not recovery authority. Negative publication evidence: `TestSidechatWorktreeBorrowing`; API/runtime regression: `TestSessionsV3PlanSidechatWorktreeRuntimeIdentity`. Live UI retry remains unverified.
- **Plan sidebar borrowed lane:** `api/sessions_v3_primary.go:inheritSessionsV3SystemSidechatWorkspace` copies parent owner and recorded base on create/rebind. `run/service_workspace_identity.go:validateRunRepositoryIdentity` authenticates the live parent and compares inherited identity before the normal workspace catalog checks; `validateSessionExecutionRepositoryIdentity` / `worktree.ValidateOwnedExecutionIdentity` retain repository, branch and recorded-base identity checks without requiring allocation-base ancestry for execution. `validateSessionRepositoryIdentity` / `worktree.ValidateOwnedIdentity` retain the stricter ancestry gate for workspace transitions. Sidebar access is not ownership or workspace-transition permission. `sessions_v3_sidechat_worktree_test.go` exercises the handler through the real resolver, repeated legacy rebind, retained conversation, foreign principal/owner/account and stale-branch rejection. Plan tool contracts and `permission.EditPendingPlanProposal` revision/realtime authority are unchanged.

- **Authority:** `session.Service.ApplySessionMutation` -> `SessionStore.ApplyV3SessionMutation`.
- **Atomic products:** event, projection, run intent/state, message/resources, media/search indexes as applicable, and durable outbox. Commit uses synchronous Pebble batches in `session_event_store.go`.
- **Reads/sync:** `handleSessionsV3SyncBootstrap`, `handleSessionsV3SyncHydrate`, `handleSessionsV3SyncStream`; live WS is `handleV3RealtimeStream`.
- **Protocol:** `v3.realtime`, version 1 (`sessions_v3_realtime_contract.go:13-38`). Cursors are signed/scoped/opaque by contract; clients must not parse or compare cursor numbers (`V3SyncContractText`).
- **Plans:** lifecycle services are in `session/plan_lifecycle_service.go`, `api/sessions_v3_plan_lifecycle.go`, and `run/plan_lifecycle_v3.go`; plan/checkpoint writes must use the session mutation boundary.
- **Recovery:** `api/sessions_v3_executor.go`, `sessions_v3_stale_recovery.go`, and durable run-intent records recover or terminalize interrupted work.
- **Compatibility:** `/v3/sessions:workset` and `/v3/tui/sessions:workset` remain removal-gated by `V3SyncWorksetRemovalGates`. `/v1/sessions*` and `/ws` are legacy surfaces, not the canonical V3 chat path.

### Agents, models, providers, prompts, and tools

- **System agents:** immutable definitions and tool contracts live in `agent/system_agent_registry.go`. `NewSystemAgentRegistry` rejects duplicate/invalid definitions, delegation-enabled subagents, and primary agents without `plan_auto` (`lines 69-120`).
- **Current compiled identities:** 14 definitions—Swarm, Plan and AI sidechats, Compact, Finder, Coder, Designer, Image, Idea, AI Task Preparer, Review Commit, Workspace Definition, Workspace Onboarding, and Router (`builtinSystemAgentDefinitions`, `system_agent_registry.go`; IDs/names in the same file).
- **Model authority:** account-scoped `agentmodelsettings.Service`; canonical route constant `AgentModelSettingsPath = /v1/agent-model-settings` (`api/swarm_mode_settings.go:14-16`).
- **Provider boundary:** generic orchestration stays in `run/`; wire/auth/stream translation belongs in `provider/*`. Registration truth is `runtime/daemon.go:455-471`, not directory presence.
- **Prompt assembly:** `run/service_prompt.go` composes the mandatory harness and agent overlay with workspace scope, then appends backend-authoritative run/checkpoint context from `run_state_prompt.go` and `checkpoint_prompt.go`; inspect those symbols for exact ordering. Runtime and durable state injections override stale transcript claims, but this is a construction/enforcement mechanism—not a claim that arbitrary repository text is intrinsically safe.
- **Tools/permissions:** implementation is under `tool/`, `run/service_tools.go`, `run/service_workspace_scope.go`, and `permission/`. `resolveRunWorkspaceScope` authenticates the current execution/source identity and `mergeAuthorizedSessionWorkspaceGrantRoots` revalidates explicit durable session grants; it does not admit every saved catalog entry. Ordinary task targets require exact authorized roots (or authenticated retained/source aliases), not arbitrary descendants or sibling worktree paths. `committed_source` is a separate exact-lineage authorization path, not a workspace grant; path expansion and operation policy remain independent. The compiled Coder contract disables bash, delegation, direct user input, session/plan/settings mutations, and enables dedicated Git tools (`CoderAgentToolContract`, `system_agent_registry.go:446-457`). `CoderAgentPrompt` and `masterHarnessPromptWithScope` explicitly assign implementation/test authoring and dedicated-Git handoff to Coders, and command execution/validation to the parent. Missing Bash by design is not a child blocker. The parent may batch independent bounded tests against exact committed trees, feeds failures back to focused correction waves, and must not invent a test hook between automatically scheduled Task Program stages. `TestCoderParentValidationPromptContract` checks emitted parent/Coder guidance, parsed staged examples and unchanged compiled tool restrictions; it is not provider-backed orchestration proof. A Coder receives authorized external context only as read-only roots, while any mutation outside its owned worktree scope or any unrelated read path is rejected by `ScopeExpansionForCall` before `requestWorkspaceScopePermission` can create a user-facing permission record. Designer has distinct managed-artifact and workspace-edit contracts so the same run cannot silently combine both authorities; Idea is tool-free (`DesignerAgentToolContract`, `DesignerWorkspaceAgentToolContract`, and `IdeaAgentToolContract` in `system_agent_registry.go`). Runtime profiles can narrow capabilities further.
- **Task Program authoring preflight:** `store/pebble.TaskProgramJobSpec.UnmarshalJSON` accepts the advertised `subagent_type` input alias but persists only canonical `agent_type`, rejecting conflicts and unknown job fields. `taskscope.Canonical` supplies the shared lexical grammar for plan approval, task parsing, and sparse allocation: exact relative files/directories or trailing `/**`, not filename globs. Program scopes remain explicit; workspace Designers retain concrete-only targets. Allocation validates every scope before Git mutation; typed scope errors classify as planning defects rather than inspecting filenames for “artifact”. Harness and tool-schema guidance reuse `taskscope.Guidance`. Focused parity/no-partial-launch, alias-conflict, and real-Git no-allocation tests live in `run/task_program_authoring_contract_test.go` and `worktree/task_scope_preflight_test.go`; this does not establish universal model reliability.
- **Router hydration:** `configuredTaskSwarmRouter.Hydrate` regenerates once after JSON/complete-wave validation failure from the unchanged request, with separate fresh lineage and one shared 90-second budget. Stream and final-only output are capped; transport, cancellation, tool-call and resource failures do not retry. `hydrateTaskSwarmLaunches` validates/composes the entire wave before assigning any prompts; persistent failure returns no workers. Parent brief, themes, controls and typed output contracts stay authoritative; managed native Designer prompts use `artifact_v3_author` build/finish, not legacy authoring. Tool/harness guidance forbids shortening narration, reducing count, splitting the wave or switching to regular delegation to bypass failure. Five fake-provider tests in `service_task_swarm_recovery_test.go` prove bounded recovery and unchanged failed waves; installed-provider reliability and actual Designer production remain unverified.
- **Coder dependency handoff (2026-09-10):** `run/service_task_program_dependencies.go` retains integrated-state, authorized repository-lane, clean-tree and exact-HEAD checks, but supplies Coder consumers with immutable source identity rather than serializing the cumulative program diff. `runCohort` passes `ProgramRepositoryLane`; `worktree.prepareTaskWorktreeCheckout` supplies the complete committed read tree independently of mutation scope. Other consumers retain bounded inline source and binary rejection. `TestTaskProgramRealStageUsesIntegratedBase` now asserts oversized/binary Coder handoff success, complete large-source materialization outside mutation scope, and unintegrated/stale/dirty rejection. These assertions passed twice in the 2026-09-20 focused correction regression selection; independent review and live recovery remain unproved.
- **Task Program dependency/runtime repair:** `worktree.prepareTaskWorktreeCheckout` materializes complete committed read source without changing mutation scopes; `PrepareTaskIntegration` rechecks declared scope (NUL-delimited changed paths, rename endpoints) and scheduler stage bases. `run/service_task_program_workspace.go` authorizes sources before allocation, persists immutable `TaskProgramRepositoryLane` bindings, validates deterministic ownership/common Git repository, and reuses clean integrated lanes without promoting captured checkouts. Typed scheduler-only destinations preserve approved source/write-scope identity. `session.TaskProgramRepositoryLanesForAdmission` exempts only the exact persisted scheduler admission (parent/program/run/revision/definition/lane), rejects ended owner runs and other active programs, and never reconciles records. `store.TransitionTaskProgram` rechecks competitors under session/worktree locks before lane publication; ordinary lane lookup and workspace-transition pins remain strict. `session/task_program_artifact.go` verifies child/program/job/turn lineage and exact revision evidence; native output references survive store reopen and status never reconstructs legacy IDs. Dependency hydration excludes Finder self-reports, quotes bounded source evidence, binds a single selected Designer dependency, and rejects ambiguous/unselected/binary/oversized sources. Cohorts retain parent run identity and distinct native job slots; ambient chat artifact selections do not override programs. `session.GetTaskProgram` reconciles terminal owning runs to blocked, preserving children; failed persistence retains the last scheduler snapshot. **Gaps:** program storage is still a separate revision-guarded record, not atomic with V3 outbox; process death between Git integration and store transition needs explicit committed-child reconciliation, never automatic replay. Real-provider mixed workflows, ten checkpoints, crash-window recovery and UI delivery remain live-test obligations.
- **Resolved-merge promotion:** `worktree/service.go` preserves a single unscoped descendant source with a new merge via an explicitly planned fast-forward; it does not replay the conflicting pre-resolution patch. Preparation retains captured-base ancestry and scope checks; application revalidates the plan under the integration lock. `integration_resolved_merge_test.go` verifies resolved bytes/exact HEAD and rejection without mutation for divergent conflicts, dirty/stale targets, forged plans and unowned files. Scoped/multi-source merge replay and external Git writers racing the service lock remain outside this correction. Tool and review-worktree API ownership/approval boundaries are unchanged.
- **Bounded preserved source authority and replacement allocation:** `tool/runtime_recovery_source.go` exposes `InspectRecoverySource`, `RetainRecoverySource`, and `ReadRecoverySource`. Existing recovery parent/child/catalog/lane checks authenticate an exact child. `recoverySourceLineage` uses read-only `session.InspectTaskProgram` and current job linkage for program children (including omitted call selectors); regular waves require unique launch-map membership. `recoverySourceIdentity` requires an inactive, ended lifecycle and no active V3 run intent; errored/interrupted producers qualify, and a completed producer qualifies only for a canonical blocked/failed/cancelled job. Job/run mismatch fails closed without lifecycle relabeling. Exact sorted relative regular UTF-8 files (32 files, 128 KiB total, eight retained manifests per parent) bind bytes, deletion/executable intent, HEAD/base/branch and producer generation into a SHA-256 identity. Retention uses sequence-guarded `ApplySessionMutation` metadata publication with durable V3 event/projection/outbox, never source Git writes. Consumption rechecks current source and rejects changes. Unix confined nonblocking/no-follow opens reject unsafe files; other platforms fail closed. `manage-worktree inspect_source/retain_source` returns an exact digest and bounded file metadata (not source bytes). Regular `task` launches and Task Program jobs carry `recovery_source_digest` through approval manifests and durable definitions; replacement session metadata binds it to the parent. `run/service_task_recovery_source.go` authenticates the source/target repository and original base, requires narrow owned scope, and rechecks freshness before/after initialization. `worktree.AllocateTaskWorkspaceWithSource` invokes `tool.MaterializeRecoverySource` before session creation and removes the entire allocation on initialization failure. Ignored documentation remains unstaged with parent-owned permission-gated inclusion/explicit tracked handoff guidance; there is no force-add grant. `runtime_recovery_source_test.go` exercises owned publication, changed-source rejection, account/user/session and path/binary/size negatives with unchanged parent/source postconditions. Allocation tests prove exact bytes/executable intent, deletion, scope and destination-symlink rejection, changed source, injected post-write cleanup and unchanged original Git state. Cross-session atomic producer-restart exclusion, cleanup under filesystem failure/process crash, and provider-backed replacement execution remain unproven; freshness uses bounded checks, not a producer lock.
- **Exact committed-child correction:** `tool/runtime_committed_source.go:ResolveCommittedSource` authenticates the current parent/account/user, unique successful regular handoff or completed canonical program job/run, current generation/owner, inactive lifecycle/no active intent, exact catalog/repository identity, clean full HEAD and base ancestry. Regular Coder-only `committed_source={task_call_id,child_session_id,head_commit}` survives parser/approval binding and execution rechecks (`service_task_launch.go`, `service_tools.go`); workspace overrides, recovery digests, swarms and program job declarations reject. A fresh full-tree checkout starts at C, including binary/executable/out-of-scope read prerequisites; existing mutation scope stays unchanged. Child/parent metadata retain C separately from inherited delivery base B and original destination. `authenticateLineageDeliveryBase` verifies the prior recorded chain before tool integration/promotion delivers B..H. Owned secondary program lanes authenticate their own catalog identity, never the parent's primary ID. Recall exposes validated tuples and promotion-only captured destinations without granting integration or advancing checkouts. Parent `task_launches` publication now uses V3 metadata mutation/CAS with bounded retry; publication failure stops dispatch and preserves registered inactive children. Full source-worktree availability is required; this does not recreate removed program worktrees. Workflow: [committed-child correction](testing/committed-child-correction.md). Git/child/generation/publication are separate boundaries, not one crash-atomic transaction; no automatic same-call retry or destructive cleanup is implied.
- **Agent recovery inventory:** `run/service_workspace_recovery.go:selectRecoveryPaths` limits inspection to 100 sorted registrations and reports `truncated` with exact-`worktree_path` retry guidance. `DiscoverRecoveryPaths` reads only byte/time-bounded registration metadata; `InspectRecoveryOwnership` authorizes each selected path before Git/content inspection. Unknown provenance is excluded; owned unreadable lanes return diagnostics without usable fresh recovery identity. Attack points are selector normalization, foreign ownership and partial inventory mistaken for exhaustive results; focused `service_workspace_discovery_bounds_test.go` and `recovery_discovery_bounds_test.go` assert these boundaries. Live recovery and independent test review remain unproven here.
- **Managed-lane manual recovery:** `tool/runtime_worktree_recovery.go` authenticates current parent/principal and independent Coder lineage before owned-lane resolution selects the exact primary/retained lane or persisted `TaskProgramRepositoryLane`. The committed-source destination resolver additionally classifies an exact authorized regular captured destination as promotion-only for recall; integration still rejects it. Source authorization queries the exact recorded `lane.SourcePath` through `workspace.ScopeForPathForPrincipal` and requires matched canonical primary/resolved path equality. The flat account catalog revalidates saved directory identity; cached active roots, legacy `Directories`, broader saved ancestors and promotion-target fallback cannot grant recovery authority or bypass revocation. Runtime roots never widen. `ValidateTaskRepositoryLane` checks deterministic owner path, common repository, branch and clean destination; captured program-base ancestry is rechecked. Recall evaluates commits in each child's destination repository and preserves dirty children without a recorded terminal HEAD. Whole-wave failures remain errors; an explicit committed sibling selection can recover without replay. `session.TaskProgramRepositoryLanes` is read-only and rejects active programs; only the scheduler's exact-admission method excludes itself. Explicit integration `workspace_path` must match the authenticated child destination or its exact saved source; it cannot retarget a batch and is no longer ignored. `runtime_manage_worktree_recovery_test.go` proves mixed-wave rejection/no-partial-write, successful-sibling recovery, primary-lane idempotency, cross-principal/foreign-parent rejection, stale/forged paths and identities, dirty destinations, and saved-source authorization/revocation/failure using the real workspace service/store with independently saved A/B entries and an A-only parent source scope, plus temporary managed Git/Pebble fixtures. Original-session integration and runtime rollout are not proved by these tests.
- **Designer cohort workspace selection:** `executeTaskTool` and `buildTaskLaunchPermissionPayload` use `retainTaskResolvedWorkspace` to retain resolved explicit selectors only for Coder/Finder. A managed Designer's implicit parent read context is not persisted as a forbidden explicit target; explicit Designer workspace selectors still reject at `resolveTaskTargetWorkspace`. The repeated-admission regression is `service_task_designer_target_test.go`.
- **Task Program child assignment boundary:** `executeTaskTool` calls `taskChildAssignmentPrompt` for program jobs: the hydrated meta-prompt and exact dependency report remain the child assignment; the parent-level launch/recovery imperative is not appended as a competing task. `buildTaskDelegationPrompt` also emits exact canonical `owned_scope` JSON paths for Coder/Finder, with unchanged mutation enforcement and read-only Finder guidance. `service_task_owned_scope_delivery_test.go` checks parsed/durable/allocation/prompt equality and neighboring unowned-path rejection; provider consumption remains a separate proof. Regular launches retain their existing prompt composition. `service_task_child_assignment_test.go` checks exact evidence preservation and exclusion of the conflicting parent imperative; a separate live two-Finder chain on `be7762e5` consumed the report without attempting parent orchestration.
- **Finder workspace identity:** `prepareDelegatedSubagentLaunchWithProfile` clears inherited parent worktree identity and temporary roots when the resolved Finder target differs from the parent execution root. `resolveRunWorkspaceScope` must then use that authorized target, not silently redirect reads to the parent; same-root Finder inheritance remains intact. Focused persisted-session/source-read and foreign-path expansion assertions are in `service_task_finder_workspace_test.go`; corrected-candidate live replay remains required.
- **Delegation forms:** regular dependency-ready waves are handled by task launch code; Task Programs schedule declared stage/job dependencies and terminalize failures/blockers durably (`service_task_program*.go`); Iteration Swarms fan out Designer/Image/Idea alternatives with mode-specific routing (`service_task_swarm.go`). Child session lineage and generation rotation are persisted in `store/pebble/delegated_child_rotation_store.go`.

### Workspaces, worktrees, actions, discovery, Git

- Catalog creation via `manage_workspace` now requires the canonical local topology binding through `workspace.Service.CreateCatalogEntryForPrincipal`, wired by `api.Server.SetTopologyService`. Binding failure rolls back the newly created catalog entry; account selection remains unchanged. Desktop overview projects the persisted binding into session route authority; it must not fabricate missing authority. Previously catalog-only entries can be explicitly re-added through `/v1/workspace/add` to retain their identity and establish the missing binding. Desktop reports routing unavailable rather than no selection. Regression source: `api/workspace_catalog_binding_test.go` (binding projection, duplicate preservation, account isolation, failure rollback and explicit repair) and `run/service_workspace_manage_worktree_test.go` (tool creation persists a real binding). Tests authored, not executed; live rebuild/session creation still requires verification.

- Runtime revocation: `run.mergeAuthorizedSessionWorkspaceGrantRoots` omits missing/account-inaccessible or inactive **additional** catalog grants without changing captured history. `sessionsV3InitialWorkspaceGrants` captures active catalog entries at admission; guarded catalog deletion removes catalog authority, not files or session records. Required primary/source identity and live grant generation/path mismatches still reject. `service_workspace_revocation_test.go` exercises ordinary and real managed-lane deletion, foreign access, stale identity, unchanged session data and tool expansion; this is hermetic scope evidence, not live provider/UI proof.

- Workspace records/bindings are Pebble-backed (`store/pebble/workspace_store.go`). The account Workspace Map is a separate lower-authority, revisioned orientation document (`store/pebble/workspace_map_store.go`) injected only into the primary Swarm prompt; `manage_workspace update_map` builds backend-owned canonical approved arguments, requires action-specific `workspace_map_update` authorization, and executes with optimistic revision matching (`run/service_workspace_manage.go`, `run/service_tools.go`). Path containment is a security boundary; inspect `workspace/` and `worktree.pathWithinRoot` before accepting a path.
- `workspace.Service.InspectRepositoryForPrincipal` is the saved-workspace prerequisite authority: add, select, path-changing update, linked-directory compatibility, and `manage_workspace create` all require the exact canonical repository root with a resolvable initial commit before catalog/topology/current-session mutation. `/v1/workspace/repository/setup` initializes empty canonical projects or the verified non-root runtime user's private writable home, and acknowledges unchanged ready retries. Home setup uses an explicitly empty tree and initial-HEAD CAS, never imports existing files or the index, and rejects root, mismatched account, stale and symlink selections. Both onboarding clients disclose omitted content and pass committed-only consent when saving after setup. Authenticated `/v1/onboarding` guidance exposes `home_path`, not an invented project destination. Nonempty/unborn projects use authenticated `/repository/review` and `/repository/baseline`: bounded exact-byte review, explicit selection/omission consent, private index, initial-only HEAD CAS and durable commit receipt, without changing user index/history or saving catalog state. Effective-daemon access checks include Git/common metadata; trust, access, absent Git and command failures stay distinct. New folders may use any accessible canonical parent, not only daemon home. Add requires `confirm_committed_only` when ignored/uncommitted content would be omitted; `content_ready` is distinct from usable committed HEAD. Desktop add/onboarding surfaces consume typed `workspace_repository_not_ready` state and no longer expose a temporary non-repository session path.
- TUI first-workspace controls (`internal/ui/onboarding_repository.go`, `internal/app/onboarding_discovery.go`) offer Use home folder or Create a new project folder (recommended), without implicitly selecting launch CWD. `internal/ui/onboarding_project.go` collects a blank project name and editable parent, previews the destination, and retains the in-memory draft on Back. Destination inspection precedes folder/Git/empty-first-commit consent. Persistent exit/relaunch draft recovery remains unimplemented. Existing repositories are loaded on request through authenticated `/v1/workspace/discover` (limit 100), supplemented by known Git directories; choosing one only requests repository inspection. No-results guidance offers path entry or new creation, not a Git prerequisite dead end. Default discovery searches daemon-account locations, not arbitrary users' homes; additional locations remain explicitly selectable.
- Desktop folder continuation (`desktop-onboarding-gate.tsx`, `workspace-folder-tree.tsx`, `workspace-editor-modal.tsx`, `workspace-home-page.tsx`) offers the same home/new-project guidance and blank-name/parent/destination form. Onboarding confirms folder creation plus empty starting commit together, then separately awaits catalog addition and finalization; other folder surfaces retain their own explicit actions. Failures retain the selected path and expose recheck/change-folder controls; setup does not imply successful registration or finalization. Onboarding assistance is offered only for `needs_assisted_setup`, while missing Git and missing HEAD have distinct remediation. `workspace-onboarding-recovery.spec.ts` exercises actual React handlers with mocked APIs in headless Chromium; `repository_onboarding_recovery_test.go` adds missing-Git and command-failure rollback negatives. These are interaction/service evidence, not production styling or live-provider proof.

- Memory metadata (`store/pebble/memory_metadata.go`): optional `purpose` (preference/project_context/operational_context/recovery/orientation) and bounded descriptive `subject` are independent of trust `kind` and server-owned creation `origin` (user/learned/system; absent legacy origin stays unknown). `prepareMutation` preserves creation origin on replacement; restore retains historical metadata, timestamps remain store-owned. Runtime partial edits preserve omitted fields, HTTP remember remains full replacement. Source-bearing writes additionally verify session account/user; automated grant/event checks remain in `FinishMemoryBatch`. Explicit HTTP authoring still cannot forge source arrays. No metadata grants access or automatically creates a rule.
- Blocked plan summaries (`session.SummarizePlanExecution`) expose `waiting_reason` from the existing durable checkpoint handoff overview and one `resolution_action` from its recommendation. Only blocked checkpoints project these fields, with auto-advance disabled; missing legacy evidence stays absent. Review/failure and pending permissions remain separate authorities. `plan_waiting_test.go` exercises active and sequential selection; Desktop `desktop-v3-cache-selectors.ts` places blocked plans in a distinct Blocked group before Needs Review, and labels failed attempts FAILED rather than BLOCKED. `session-plan-record.ts` retains the durable handoff overview as waitingReason (the checkpoint handoff is not a schema-versioned final card); `desktop-plan-execution-sidebar.tsx` shows that reason and recommendation action without a permission grant or direct unblock control. `runtime/blocker-transitions.ts` feeds existing Desktop toasts from canonical sidebar snapshots: initial hydration is silent, duplicate snapshots and temporary reconnect gaps retain attempt identity, and permissions/errors alone never notify. These are in-app transition notices, not new persisted notification-center records or background push. Focused selector/tracker/SSR tests pass; live transport and browser pixel evidence remain unverified.
- Explicit AI memory objects: `run.executeMemoryTool` exposes inspect, remember (full-object put), edit (existing-object partial update), and permanent forget. Remember requires entry ID/content/kind; missing fields now return actionable policy errors rather than an unexplained rejection. Edit preserves omitted scope/pin/provenance and commits through account document CAS; it cannot create a missing object or resurrect a forgotten ID. Mutation approval remains required even in bypass mode. HTTP authoring retains its existing `remember` full-replacement contract. `service_memory_operations_test.go` exercises CRUD, rejection postconditions, foreign accounts, restricted agents and permission admission. The recovered failed-call arguments confirm omitted entry ID with learned content, explicit intent and current revision; the regression reproduces that shape and requires an entry-ID-specific error with unchanged state. Corrected-build live execution remains unverified.
- Restricted memory automation: `memory.Service` exposes authenticated remember/configure/run-now/cancel operations; `RunScheduler` schedules opted-in owners serially. `MemoryStore.ClaimMemoryJob` reads eligible visible messages incrementally, with a resumable account-index position (`memory_scan.go`), message sequences and UTF-8 fragment offsets. No moving lookback cutoff discards unread messages. Each batch contains at most 12,000 source bytes/32 fragments; successful batches atomically publish multiple learned entries and progress through `FinishMemoryBatch`/`prepareMutation`. Unfinished scans become due again; failures retain the last committed position. Job history retains 64 records rather than imposing a lifetime run cap. Settings changes, cancellation and live source revocation prevent publication; legacy uncommitted review proposals are interrupted and reread. `RuntimeProvider` has no tools, workspace, invoker or continuation. There is no monetary budget, pricing admission, review approval or verified-output-ceiling prerequisite. Normal provider model/context behavior remains adapter-owned; decoded output is bounded. Explicit rules and pinned entries remain protected. Account-owner enumeration is still capped at 256 and requires future pagination evidence for larger installations.
- Workspace records/bindings are Pebble-backed (`store/pebble/workspace_store.go`). The account Workspace Map is a lower-authority revisioned compatibility view (`store/pebble/workspace_map_store.go`) over the single account memory authority (`store/pebble/memory_store.go`); the first memory access atomically preserves legacy map content/revision and deletes its old key. `MemoryStore` synchronously batches account CAS, distinct user rule/orientation versus learned ownership, bounded settings/content/history, provenance, redacted forgetting tombstones and new-revision restore. Reading/explicit remember default on; automation defaults off/manual; opted-in learning applies automatically. UTF-8 byte upper-bound accounting is explicit. Tombstones are permanent and bounded (capacity fails closed); exclusions remain forgotten even after the exclusion is removed. Learned retention is applied before reads, including historical snapshots. Primary Swarm now uses `SelectMemory` through `accountMemoryPromptBlock` on each provider step: whole entries sorted rule/pinned/orientation/learned then stable ID, serialized metadata counted against the injection budget, scoped context and explicit omissions. Cross-workspace orientation is contextual only. `/memory` displays the same authenticated next-request selection, clearly not historical provider input. Read-time source deletion also redacts live/history-only copies. `manage_memory` supplies principal-bound inspect/remember/edit/forget with CAS, intent and mutation permission;  `manage_workspace update_map` builds backend-owned canonical approved arguments, requires action-specific `workspace_map_update` authorization, and executes with optimistic revision matching (`run/service_workspace_manage.go`, `run/service_tools.go`). Path containment is a security boundary; inspect `workspace/` and `worktree.pathWithinRoot` before accepting a path.
- `workspace.Service.InspectRepositoryForPrincipal` is the saved-workspace prerequisite authority: add, select, path-changing update, linked-directory compatibility, and `manage_workspace create` all require the exact canonical repository root with a resolvable initial commit before catalog/topology/current-session mutation. `/v1/workspace/repository/setup` is the only automatic setup path and may initialize only an empty unsaved canonical directory, with stale-path and symlink guards; non-empty directories require explicit file/ignore review and user-authorized Git mutations. Desktop add/onboarding surfaces consume typed `workspace_repository_not_ready` state and no longer expose a temporary non-repository session path.
- Desktop folder continuation (`desktop-onboarding-gate.tsx`, `workspace-folder-tree.tsx`, `workspace-editor-modal.tsx`, `workspace-home-page.tsx`) separates folder creation, confirmed repository setup and catalog addition. Failures retain the selected path and expose recheck/change-folder controls; setup does not imply successful registration or finalization. Onboarding assistance is offered only for `needs_assisted_setup`, while missing Git and missing HEAD have distinct remediation. `workspace-onboarding-recovery.spec.ts` exercises actual React handlers with mocked APIs in headless Chromium; `repository_onboarding_recovery_test.go` adds missing-Git and command-failure rollback negatives. These are interaction/service evidence, not production styling or live-provider proof.
- `worktree.Service.AllocateTaskWorkspace` owns delegated checkout isolation; integration validates child path, branch, base, and head (`worktree/service.go`). `ResolveTaskBase`, `resolveRepositoryRoot`, and `resolveRepositoryHeadCommit` are the downstream pre-allocation boundary: missing Git, a plain directory, and an unborn repository fail distinctly before a managed path or branch is created. The `/v1/worktrees` read path propagates these errors rather than downgrading the workspace to direct-session mode.
- Workspace Action definition CRUD (`action.Service.Create/Update/Delete/Reorder`) is separate from execution (`action.Runner`; `api/workspace_actions.go` and `workspace_action_runs.go`). Entrypoints resolve relative to the real workspace and arguments are structured arrays (`action/runner.go:100-104,288-341`).
- Rules/skills are discovered in `discovery.Service`; they guide agents but cannot override runtime policy.
- Git status/watch is in `gitstatus/` and `gitwatch/`; authenticated API mutation surfaces include status, commit, suggestion, realtime, and worktree management.

### Environments, connections, and reusable deployments

- **Substrate architecture (`Connection -> Environment -> Deployment -> DeploymentLease -> Consumer`):** Reusable execution environments separate target hosts, declarative environment templates, instantiated containers, and atomic consumer leases into distinct workspace-scoped entities (`pkg/environments/`).
  - **Connection (`pkg/environments/connection.go`):** Identifies the target runtime host (`local_docker`, `ssh_docker`). Stores host address, port, user, and socket path. Strictly data-only configuration; secrets (passwords, private SSH keys, API tokens) are strictly forbidden in Pebble and must rely on system SSH agent/config or runtime authentication.
  - **Environment (`pkg/environments/environment.go`):** Declarative template for containerized workloads (Docker image, Dockerfile build context, source provisioning via `local_mount` or `remote_existing_path`, exposed/forwarded ports, environment variables, resource limits, `release_behavior`: `none`, `restart`, `recreate`, `max_instances`, `reuse` flag, and `preferred_connection_id`). Strictly free of ephemeral runtime state (no container IDs, dynamic IPs, or assigned host ports).
  - **Deployment (`pkg/environments/deployment.go`):** Instantiated runtime instance on a concrete connection host. Stores ephemeral runtime metadata (`container_id`, `provider_resource_id`, primary `endpoint`, `assigned_ports`, `remote_workspace_path`, `runtime_ip`, `engine_version`, status, health, and lifecycle timestamps).
  - **DeploymentLease (`pkg/environments/lease.go`):** Exclusive atomic ownership tracking for safe concurrent isolation. Scoped to account + workspace + deployment. Records `acquired_at`, `released_at`, `expires_at` (TTL in ms; 0 = explicit release), `active` boolean, `release_reason`, and generic consumer ownership fields (`consumer_type`: `session`, `test_run`, `worker`, `custom`; `consumer_id`; `consumer_metadata`).
  - **Consumer:** Arbitrary execution entity holding an active lease: interactive agent/chat sessions, automated test execution runs, background Worker V2 runs, or custom pipelines.
- **Pebble persistence (`swarmd/internal/store/pebble/`):**
  - Key prefixes: `ConnectionStore` (`env:conn:`), `EnvironmentStore` (`env:def:`), `DeploymentStore` (`env:dep:`), `LeaseStore` (`env:lease:`). All records enforce strict `accountScopeID:workspaceID:` prefixing and tenant isolation.
  - Active lease indexing (`env:lease:active:...` and `env:lease:dep:active:...`) ensures O(1) active lease lookups and race-free concurrency enforcement.
  - Workspace settings in `workspace_store.go` store `default_connection_id` and `default_test_environment_id`.
- **Deployment providers (`swarmd/internal/environments/provider/`):**
  - `DeploymentProvider` interface defines lifecycle (`ValidateConnection`, `Capabilities`, `Deploy`, `Inspect`, `Start`, `Stop`, `Destroy`), runtime access resolution (`ResolveAccess`), and command execution (`Exec`).
  - `LocalDockerProvider`: Communicates with local Docker daemon via socket/pipe or CLI, supports port mapping, volume binds (`local_mount`), env overrides, health check inspection, port inspection, and container `exec`.
  - `SSHDockerProvider`: Manages remote Docker containers over persistent SSH connections, supports remote port forwarding, remote workspace paths (`remote_existing_path`), remote Docker exec, and remote port inspection.
- **Lifecycle manager (`swarmd/internal/environments/lifecycle/`):**
  - `DeploymentManager` coordinates 3-tier connection resolution: 1. explicit request override, 2. Environment `preferred_connection_id`, 3. Workspace `default_connection_id`.
  - `EnsureDeployment`: Atomically acquires an unleased usable deployment if `reuse=true`, or provisions a new deployment via provider `Deploy` subject to `max_instances` limits; acquires an atomic `DeploymentLease` and returns deployment, lease, and resolved `DeploymentAccess`.
  - `ReleaseDeployment`: Atomically releases the lease, marks it inactive with `release_reason`, and executes the environment's configured `ReleaseBehavior` (`none`: leave running for instant reuse; `restart`: restart container; `recreate`: stop, destroy, and re-provision clean container).
  - Background TTL sweep (`StartLeaseSweeper`) automatically reclaims expired leases and triggers release behavior.
- **AI Tools and prompt injection (`swarmd/internal/tool/`, `swarmd/internal/run/`):**
  - Tools: `manage_connections` (list, get, create, update, delete, test), `manage_environments` (list, get, create, update, delete, set_default_test), and `manage_deployments` (ensure, deploy, list, get, start, stop, destroy, inspect, access, exec, lease, release, reap).
  - Permission actions: `manage_connections_view`, `manage_connections_manage`, `manage_environments_view`, `manage_environments_manage`, `manage_deployments_view`, `manage_deployments_lease`, `manage_deployments_manage`.
  - Prompt injection (`service_environments_context.go`): Injects concise default testbench context (`[default-test-environment]`) when configured, allowing agents to automatically discover and lease the workspace's target test environment for E2E and unit test execution without user intervention.
- **Desktop UI (`web/src/features/desktop/environments/`):**
  - Route: `/$workspaceSlug/environments` with tabbed views for Environments, Connections, and Deployments.
  - Interactive modals for Connection creation/testing, Environment definition creation/editing (with presets for Node, Go, Python, Rust, Postgres, Redis), Deployment detail and runtime access inspection, and command execution drawer.

### Shared session execution capacity

`permission.Policy.ActiveExecutionLimit` is the account-wide execution ceiling (default 100 when absent, validated range 1–10000); it does not replace existing approval policy or usage limits. `permission.Service.ExecutionCapacity` owns one live `executioncapacity.Manager` shared by `sessionV3Executor.run`, delegated `run.Service.runTurn`, and direct `RunManualCompaction`. Ordinary, deployed and delegated sessions consume the same pool. Synchronous task/permission waits park the owner while retaining session serialization; internal compaction borrows only an active same-account/session context lease. Standalone compaction acquires normally. Pending child stop cancels admission before provider/message execution.

The manager bounds waiters, reserves parked-owner reacquisition, and signals release/policy changes without polling. Live leases are not durable authority: canonical V3 pending run intents remain recoverable; the executor bounds in-flight dispatch, coalesces paged refill, and reconciles interrupted running intents at restart without replaying work. Shutdown joins the refill pump and pending execution. `manage-sessions inspect` and runtime instructions report shared active/deployed counts, available slots, the eight-proposal batch bound and no configured saved-session quota. `pending` explicitly means live admission waiters, not all durable overflow. Snapshots do not reserve slots; missing/invalid capacity authority is unavailable, not fabricated availability. Durable watch/wait subscriptions and exact-batch follow-ups are separate, not implemented by this capacity foundation.

### Desktop and TUI ownership

- **Integration-error help:** `desktop-app-page.tsx` routes both Git sidebar and Review Worktrees help through `openIntegrationHelpDraft` to the failed work's existing session. The complete prompt is scoped to that session, appended via `DesktopV3ExistingConversationComposer`, then acknowledged and cleared so returning to the route cannot replay it. The help gesture neither creates a session nor sends a message, integrates, or archives; ordinary V3 send and Git permission boundaries remain authoritative. Likely regressions are cross-session draft leakage, overwriting a draft, replay after navigation, and accidental automatic submission. Sidebar/review specs inspect this wiring; browser delivery/focus remains a manual testing gap.

- **Desktop:** backend-derived V3 state is owned by `web/src/features/desktop/state/desktop-v3-cache-store.ts` and `desktop-v3-cache-reducer.ts`; realtime orchestration is in `realtime/v3-realtime-controller.ts`; runtime composition is under `runtime/`. Components should consume selectors/actions rather than create another transport cache. Sync shell metadata retains delegated children’s allowlisted `parent_task_call_id`, and the sidebar uses that durable identity to keep siblings from one task call adjacent inside one visual group without consulting transcript content (`sessions_v3_sync_bootstrap.go`, `sidebar-session-lineage.ts`, `desktop-app-page.tsx`).
- **Desktop assistant ordering:** `sessionV3DurableProgressSink.persistEpoch` retains each stream's first successful durable text event sequence; `sessionV3AssistantResponse.metadata` carries it as `stream_start_seq` on pre-tool and final messages through canonical V3 mutation and hydration. `applyStreamAwareDurableAssistantDelta` adopts that durable anchor once, while `buildDesktopV3ConversationRenderItems` uses the same stream/run key for draft, retained segment and committed message, suppressing replays by identity rather than matching unrelated text. Message `global_seq` remains the persistence/pagination sequence. Historical messages without the new metadata retain canonical commit ordering; no backfill is implied. Regression assertions cover tool boundaries, overlapping hydration, same-text distinct streams, malformed anchors, conflicting byte overlap and failed persistence. Seven focused Desktop tests pass, including all 124 legal interleavings of a bounded live/durable trace with batching and duplicate replay through `DesktopV3LivePatchCoordinator`; identity, placement and content are asserted at each transition. Four regressions fail against pre-fix source. Three focused backend tests pass five repetitions and three race-detector repetitions after binding fixtures to the canonical account fake-provider assignment. This is not browser DOM/scroll or deployed-build evidence.
- **Desktop realtime liveness:** `session-v3/transport.ts` (`DesktopV3RealtimeTransport.attachSocket`) checks generation and socket ownership before close-event timer cleanup. A replaced socket's asynchronous close must not cancel the current socket's existing 45-second inactivity recovery. `requestRehydrate` still uses the controller's canonical sync/reconnect path; no new timeout, transport or state authority was added. `session-v3/transport-liveness.spec.ts` exercises delayed closes after cursor-error, slow-consumer and durable-cursor recovery, owned-close reconnect, stop cleanup and explicit stale state after recovery failure.
- **User-message presentation continuity:** `DesktopV3RenderItemView` uses `desktopV3UserMessageElement` to return the same immediate user-bubble component for pending and committed rows, retaining the canonical message-ID row key rather than replacing wrapper types on acknowledgement. The routed pending shell reserves the same stable scrollbar gutters as the conversation scroller to avoid a horizontal handoff shift. `desktop-v3-user-message-transition.spec.ts` authors identity, overlap, failure/retry, and duplicate-text cases; not executed in this change. New-session route components still transition at durable activation; browser paint continuity is not yet established.
- **First-message sidebar visibility:** `useNativeArtifactCatalog` starts a background catalog request at durable session activation and during refresh. `DesktopV3ExistingConversationPane` uses `desktopV3HasArtifactSidebarContent` (`desktop-sidebar-display.ts`) to open the artifact sidebar only for catalog entries or a native catalog error, never loading alone. Empty discovery no longer opens then closes a column; existing artifacts remain visible while refreshing and native error/retry presentation remains available. The focused display spec authors empty activation/refresh, content retention, error/recovery and wiring checks; not executed and not browser-paint evidence.
- **Continuous Desktop start presentation:** `app/router.tsx` mounts workspace and session chat URLs under one pathless conversation owner. `DesktopV3ConversationPane` retains the new-start owner through its exact durable activation key; `DesktopV3NewSessionPane` renders local submitted messages in `DesktopV3ExistingConversationPane`, not the pending Router shell. The canonical `/v3/sessions:routed` transaction and authority validation remain unchanged. Atomic first-message application precedes URL replacement; hydration is background repair rather than a blocking opening screen. Local transcript row and scroll identity survive activation; switching to an unrelated session still resets ownership. `desktop-launch-canonical.e2e.spec.ts` now asserts retained scroller DOM across a held routed response and absence of a Router-status page; not executed, so browser paint continuity remains unverified.
- **Routed first user message:** `sessionsV3SyncShellMetadataKeyAllowed` must retain `routed_start` across bootstrap/hydrate; stripping it disables Desktop retention after the session shell replaces the routed response. Internal `routed_start_request_hash` remains excluded. Routed starts atomically return their first durable user message; Desktop applies it before hydration, and transcript replacement retains that initiating message for `routed_start` sessions if a later Router/run hydrate omits it, while ordinary session snapshots retain authoritative replacement semantics (`session-v3/new-session-flow.ts`, `state/desktop-v3-cache-reducer.ts`).
- **TUI:** `internal/app/app.go` owns app lifecycle. `tuiRealtimeController` in `internal/app/tui_realtime_controller.go` reconciles V3 subscriptions/worksets and resume cursor; `internal/client/` implements daemon calls.
- **Workspace-aware `/task`:** both clients call `/v3/sessions:background-router`. With no selector, TUI resolves the current canonical source workspace and Desktop resolves the active workspace; `--workspace` accepts only an available saved workspace identity. Clients send that workspace's bound self-host authority, while Router receives only task text and returns title/worktree-name seed. The backend replay-checks first, calls Router once, allocates a mandatory new owned worktree in the selected repository, and atomically publishes session/message/run intent. TUI ownership is `internal/app/task_command.go` plus `internal/client/workspace_ai_task.go`; Desktop ownership is `chat/services/task-workspace-selection.ts`, `layout/desktop-app-page.tsx`, and `session-v3/write-api.ts`.
- Both surfaces must repair gaps from durable sync. UI-local state and in-memory hubs are not session authority.

### Embedded model snapshot

- `scripts/update-model-snapshot.sh` fetches and verifies the public version/snapshot pair before installing `swarmd/internal/model/snapshotdata/{snapshot,snapshot-version}.json`; `model/snapshot_embed.go` embeds those bytes and `decodeSwarmSnapshotRecords` in `model/catalog_service.go` hydrates provider recommendations. The pinned snapshot is `509b5ba697355d46`, generated 2026-09-14, with 734 models, seven providers and six hydrated providers. Fireworks recommends DeepSeek V4.1 Flash for plan/auto and GLM 5.3 Flash for utility/finder/coder/designer/router; OpenAI and Codex recommend GPT-6 Astra for plan/designer, GPT-5.6 Sol for auto/coder with coder thinking medium, and GPT-5.6 Luna for utility/finder/router; Codex router also uses low thinking. Anthropic uses Claude Fable 5.1 for plan/auto/coder; Google uses Gemini 3.8 Flash except compact; OpenRouter recommends GPT-6 Astra for plan/coder, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash for auto, Gemini 3.8 Flash for finder/designer and Gemini 3.5 Flash Lite for router. Snapshot refresh does not itself rebuild the daemon or change saved account assignments.

### Artifacts, media, image, HTML, video, voice

- **Verified legacy/read implementation (V1).** Managed artifact authority and lineage are in `artifact/authority*.go`; bytes/manifests are in private `artifactgit` repositories. Workspace materialization must pass destination containment (`artifact/materialize.go`). Legacy Desktop viewing still infers turns, progress, and accepted heads from V1 collection/variant/step/composition records (`artifact-api.ts`, `artifact-studio-model.ts`, `desktop-v3-artifact-sidebar.tsx`, `desktop-v3-artifact-gallery.tsx`). New managed Designer work is separately projected through Artifact V2 in `artifact-v2-api.ts`, `artifact-v2-studio-model.ts`, `desktop-v3-artifact-v2-sidebar.tsx`, and `desktop-v3-artifact-v2-studio.tsx`; V1 remains a deletion/quarantine and bounded legacy-read target, not its implementation scaffold.
- Pre-session media ingress is `mediastaging.Service`: put/read/delete/expire, bounded `CleanupExpired`, account-preflighted `CleanupAbandoned`, and atomic binding (`mediastaging/service.go:50-196`).
- Image generation is `imagegen.Service`. `ManagedImageCapabilities` issues a capability snapshot/token and generation validates it; managed parallelism is bounded to 4, Gemini slots to 10 (`imagegen/service.go:35-36,197-252,568`; `gemini.go:322`).
- Video generation and conversational iteration is `videogen.Service`. Initial generation routes to configured base video generation models (Google Veo 3.1 or OpenRouter), supporting text prompts as well as image-to-video with workspace image paths, data URIs, session media assets, or artifact references; remix/iteration with video source references routes to configured conversational iteration models (Gemini Omni Flash). Managed video parallelism is bounded to 4 slots (`videogen/service.go`). Tool invocation `manage_artifact generate_video` creates managed artifact variants with lineage and interaction ID tracking (`tool/runtime_manage_artifact.go`). UI settings in `uisettings.Service` and pebble store `ui_chat_settings_store.go` configure `tools.video.default_model` and `tools.video.iteration_model`, exposed via `/v1/media/settings/catalog`.
- Audio generation and multi-turn iteration is `audiogen.Service`. Generation routes to configured or requested audio models via the Google Interactions API (defaulting to `google/lyria-3-clip-preview` for clips <= 30 seconds at $0.04/gen, and `google/lyria-3.5` for full songs > 30 seconds at $0.08/gen, or user-configured/explicit models such as `lyria-3-pro-preview`). Prompts are shaped with arrangement timestamp markers (`[00:00] ... [mm:ss]`) to guide musical cadence. Exact duration normalization and post-generation trimming are handled via `audiogen.TrimAudioToDuration` using FFmpeg with a smooth 0.5s audio fade-out (`afade=t=out`), falling back gracefully to untrimmed audio if FFmpeg is unavailable. Multi-turn conversational iteration passes `previous_interaction_id` to Google Lyria and preserves artifact lineage (`source_session_id`, `source_collection_id`, `source_variant_id`, `source_event_seq`, and `iteration_id`). Multimodal image inspiration (`image` or `image_path` parameters) supports JPEG/PNG and automatic SVG rasterization. Tool invocation `manage_artifact generate_audio` accepts optional `model` argument, pre-resolves account-configured default audio models (`tools.audio.default_model`), and creates managed artifact variants (`audio/mp3`, presentation kind `audio`, previewable: true) with complete pricing estimation, metadata tagging, and multi-prompt batching (`prompts` array up to 8 variations). UI settings in `uisettings.Service` and pebble store `ui_chat_settings_store.go` configure `tools.audio.default_model`, exposed via `/v1/media/settings/catalog` alongside `audio_models`, `audio_ready`, and `audio_status`. Desktop UI provides native browser playback via HTML5 `<audio controls>` in `desktop-v3-artifact-gallery.tsx` and `desktop-v3-artifact-preview-thumbnail.tsx`, along with `Music` icon indicators in `desktop-v3-artifact-sidebar.tsx` and music model selection in `media-settings-page.tsx`.
- **Verified current implementation (retained primitive candidates plus V1 callers).** HTML still capture enforces `swarm.capture/v1` and 1920x1080 (`htmlcapture/renderer.go:36,268-285`). Animation HTML declaring `swarm.animation/v1` cannot publish ready without a reviewed profile and trusted preflight; `motion_ui` additionally proves artifact-owned requestAnimationFrame playback before deterministic ready/seek/stability/viewport checks (`tool/runtime_manage_artifact_animation.go`, `htmlcapture/animation.go`). Deterministic capture enables focus emulation on each isolated capture page so background workers receive paint callbacks, then awaits paused-animation readiness and two paint callbacks before its first sample; two independent screenshots still require exact pixel equality, and visible timer mutation remains rejected (`TestAnimationCSSSeekStablePixelsAndRejectsMutation`). Production animation rendering uses two to four isolated browser pages according to the server-owned quality preset, a bounded ordered in-memory PNG window, and one persistent backpressured FFmpeg `image2pipe`. Artifact V2 may retain audited Chrome/encoding primitives only through V2-owned compiler/renderer interfaces; V1 manifests, model-authored bootstrap, tool runtime, publication flow, and prompts remain forbidden dependencies.
- Native conversion budget: `htmlcapture.AnimationFrameBudget` admits ceil(duration_ms*FPS/1000) <= 36000 at fixed 1920×1080, with duration >=100ms and integral FPS 1–60. Authoring, native conversion and renderer share this overflow-safe budget; storyboard totals sum rounded frames before state filtering. Fifteen minutes at 30 FPS costs 27000 frames; at 60 FPS it is rejected with lower-FPS/split-job guidance. The absolute duration guard is derived at 1 FPS, not a separate short ceiling. Renderer limits: 1920×1080, standard native capture three workers/six buffered frames, two encoder threads, 512 MiB MP4, 4s seek acknowledgement and 6s frame deadline; total capture timeout is max(30 minutes, 5 minutes + 250ms/frame), yielding 30 minutes for 85000ms/60 FPS and 35 minutes for 120000ms/60 FPS and 117.5 minutes for 15 minutes/30 FPS (155 minutes at the frame maximum), bounded further by caller cancellation/deadline. MP4 size is checked during frame streaming and before loading output; native derivative accumulation is capped at 512 MiB. Encoded size is content-dependent and cannot be known before rendering; oversize output fails without publication. These are resource ceilings, not a latency or successful full-encode guarantee. Exact source receipts and pending proposal CAS remain separate authorities; no new cross-store transaction or automatic acceptance/render is introduced.
- Video sources use opaque references (`videosource/`); projects/proposals/revisions and render-job creation/listing use `videoproject.Service`; background composition, cancellation, and restart recovery use `videorender.Service`; transcription/analysis is `videotranscription/`. Render jobs are V3 mutations pinned to account/user/session/project/revision plus allowlisted quality and 30/60 FPS, are admitted through a bounded concurrent service semaphore, retain durable progress/ETA/output lineage, and reuse an exact ready MP4 for the same revision/settings. `AcceptEditProposal` is a distinct operation from proposal creation. Native Artifact V3 conversion is owned by `artifactv3video.Service` plus `runtime.artifactV3VideoBridge`: the model supplies only exact V3 session/artifact/revision and project-base identity; the server authenticates the selected Git head/build/validation, resolves authored timing from authenticated HTML via `projectTiming`/`htmlcapture.AnimationTiming` (legacy undeclared sources alone receive defaults and an ephemeral CSS adapter); native boolean `ready() === true` asserts readiness without replacing manifest timing, while structured acknowledgements must match exactly; distinguishes Git SHA-1 OIDs from SHA-256 content digests, validates renderer-observed duration/FPS and PNG/MP4 container signatures, atomically stores digest-bound derivatives, and creates one pending proposal. `videorender.Service` materializes only the exact authenticated V3 MP4 derivative. New conversions/source selections require current-head authority; derivative reads use immutable owned revision evidence and exact persisted receipts, including timing/state/profile, so A remains readable after selecting B. `swarm-storyboard.json` (`swarm.artifact-storyboard/v3`) declares at most 16 ordered temporal sections and 36000 aggregate frames, each naming an exact HTML entry/state, stable ID, duration and filming requirements; spatial Part targets are rejected. Pending sections display exact PNG stills and block acceptance; native revision plans replace stable sections. Startup explicitly migrates receipt-less native media only from immutable owned video revisions, verifying exact Git/build/validation and media digests and retaining old reference event sequences; reads never reconstruct receipts from caller input. The migration is bounded and fails closed. Derivative sets publish atomically before proposal CAS: cancellation or later proposal failure may retain private complete derivatives but cannot imply proposal acceptance. Artifact V2 remains a separate restricted pending-proposal sink.
- Voice/STT is in `voice/` and provider adapters. Treat local model path resolution and uploaded audio as untrusted input.

## 8. API catalog

Workspace catalog realtime: `/v3/realtime/stream` emits cursor-only `workspace.catalog.updated` control frames to authenticated same-account workset subscribers, independent of the selected session. `sessions_v3_realtime_ws.go` owns admission/replay; `WorkspaceStore.putWorkspaceCatalogMutationAtomic` attaches catalog/index/current-binding writes to `ApplyV3SessionMutation`'s durable event/outbox batch. `workspace.Service.SetCatalogPublisher` and server startup supply post-commit wakeups. Desktop's retained realtime controller routes the signal to `workspace-catalog-refresh.ts`, which hydrates `/v1/workspace/overview?include_details=false&include_discovered=false` through the shared query cache; no discovery, Git, or per-row session reads are requested for invalidations.

### Session repository inventory

Desktop `session-repository-picker.tsx` displays exact lane rows grouped by saved workspace/source, active versus retained inspection state and program/job provenance in a bounded scrolling region. `session-repositories.ts` resolves newly hydrated child owners from canonical cache lineage and recognizes scoped `repository.allocated` task deltas; ordinary progress/token deltas do not refresh. Completion-driven invalidation retains selection and rejects late/disposed responses. Mutation eligibility additionally checks active state and exact status path; session commit dialogs recheck selected owner/path and freshness at submission, and sidebar integration rechecks current eligibility. Focused state/picker browser tests verify distinct successors, additional-workspace program labels, pagination, discovery, reconnect and stale targets. Narrow/wide rendered pagination states were inspected; installed-runtime proof remains separate.

Inventory rows expose `active` versus `retained`, base branch, owner session and optional program/job IDs. New program allocations capture source catalog ID/generation in their immutable lane; historical records are not relabeled to current defaults. `provenance_limit: 64` reports the canonical snapshot lane-history admission bound, not a pagination cap: indexed contexts remain pageable beyond it. Job-only program transitions invalidate sealed inventory cursors; identical replay does not. Canonical adoption/child mutations and existing durable task progress drive client invalidation; startup backfill and reconnect hydration repair missed delivery. Program storage and stream publication remain separate transactions, not an atomic program/outbox contract.

Live audit refinement: the exact compact session-transition allocator is recognized by the shared read validator. Shared immediate-parent lanes resolve only through same-principal durable parent identity and exact current or retained lane equality (the parent's exact worktree claim takes precedence over a child's captured-source claim) (`sessions_v3_repository_parent.go`); this creates no execution/mutation grant. Task Program admission reserves its widest declared stage before lower policy caps, rather than pinning later stages to first-stage width. Actual ready-job dependencies remain scheduler-owned. Matrix A5 retains the initial sequential flow and inventory failures. Its V6 reconciliation records later exact-build overlapping Coders, retained dirty recall, unsupported-plan rejection and full-app pointer/reload evidence, with explicit output corrections and remaining gaps.

`GET /v3/sessions/{id}/repositories` is registered in `handleSessionV3PrimaryByID` and served by `handleSessionV3Repositories`. It is a read-only, account/user/parent-scoped paginated view, consumed by Desktop Git inventory and attachment header through scoped durable-event invalidation and explicit refresh, not recurring polling. Completion-driven scheduling prevents unrelated V3 traffic from continually disabling Git operations. `session.RepositoryHistory`, `ExactRepositoryHistory` and `RepositoryContinuation` wrap Pebble's retained context/claim/lane indexes. Startup runs resumable 100-row maintenance batches; reads never migrate or reconcile Task Programs. Full continuations bind principal, parent, meaningful inventory revision and page limit. Pages cap at 20 rows/context reads, sequential repository inspection, a 10-second request context, 2 MiB status output and 500 returned files per row. Unavailable, revoked, missing or mismatched roots remain explicit errors, not clean repositories.

`git_snapshot.go`, `git_realtime.go` and `git_commit.go` use exact selected identity rather than silently substituting the primary lane. Historical inventory grants inspection only; commits require current authority. `ValidateSessionRepositoryLaneForRead` / `ValidateTaskRepositoryLaneForRead` preserve allocation, common-repository and branch checks while allowing dirty reads; transition validators still require cleanliness. Actual assertions inspected/executed: `sessions_v3_repositories_test.go`, `sessions_v3_repository_parent_test.go`, `session_repository_history_test.go`, `session_lane_test.go`, Desktop `session-repositories.spec.ts` and attachment/picker browser fixtures. Matrix V6 separates final-source reruns from retained exact-build provider/full-app evidence. The repository section scrolls independently and its file region does not shrink below a tall plan card; normal pointer/reload inspection, not forced clicks, establishes retained file reachability.

### How to read this catalog

All listed routes are registered by the eight `register*Routes` methods called by `Server.apiMux` (`api/server_routes.go`; `api/server.go:697-707`). Unless an exemption below applies, they inherit auth + vault + JSON middleware; Desktop access additionally inherits local-session and browser-origin boundaries. Methods are enforced inside handlers, so inspect the handler before changing or calling a route. “Tests” means related inspected files, not a coverage assertion.
