Automation V2 retirement boundary (2026-09-12): subsequent V1 automation descriptions below are retained historical architecture/evidence, not enabled runtime behavior. `runtime/daemon.go` retains only V1 read catalog/realtime composition and removes V1 execution-host/runtime/scheduler installation. V2 pending review stores the full canonical document and immutable review history under `automation/v2/`; acceptance approves its plan snapshot without setting an ordinary active-plan pointer or creating a run. Store calls require an injected canonical executable validator, invoked under the session mutation lock; production uses `validateAutomationV2Proposal`, not a weaker store validator. Exact accepted receipts remain readable after finite expiration without regranting. AI authoring now uses `tool/automation_v2_schema.go` and `run/automation_v2_tools.go`: complete schema-valid `exit_plan_mode` (Plan) or `plan_manage request_new_plan` (Auto) creates only a pending review. `gateToolCalls` checks explicit authoring denials before publication; provider dispatch checks primary/session/principal authority and terminates authoring at `await_automation_acceptance`. No waiting executable permission call or ordinary mode transition is created. `automation_v2_permission.go` commits the stable canonical PermissionRecord/pending index/summary with each proposal and resolves it with acceptance in the same batch. Bound `edit_pending_plan` uses the same exact review CAS; generic plan editing/approval cannot rewrite it. The Plan sidechat receives the permission payload's complete document/review. `manage_workers` (with `manage_automation` alias) advertises V2 reads only; legacy adapters fail closed. V2 scheduling and accepted-record edits/cancellation now use `session/automation_v2_scheduler.go`, `store/pebble/automation_v2_execution.go`, `run/automation_v2_execution.go` and `runtime/automation_v2.go`; Desktop hydration/pixels remain later integration work. `Run` installs only the V2 sweep and joins it before executor shutdown. Each admission atomically advances the accepted revision's due cursor and writes an immutable occurrence/pending index plus V3 event/outbox. Interval anchors are acceptance instants; cron evaluates explicit-zone local fields over UTC minutes (DST gap skipped, repeated minute distinct). Late sweeps skip a backlog or coalesce to one durable due slot. Indefinite has no cutoff; finite expiration rejects new admissions at the boundary. Accepted edits preserve automation identity and compare the review's base control generation so a stale card cannot undo pause; admitted snapshots remain original. Pause/resume affect future slots, cancel_future stops future admission, cancel_all fences admitted generations and awaits host acknowledgement. Execution prepares a separate managed worktree/session, journals its exact preparation before publication, installs a one-occurrence canonical plan copy and starts a durable checkpoint intent through `EnqueueAutomationRun`. Tool/turn enforcement checks immutable creation identity and current owner/cancel fence. Preparation publication and wake failures retain recoverable receipts; an allocation collision before journaling fails visibly without adopting/deleting a lane. Outcomes distinguish admitted/running, all-checkpoints-completed (not human verification), failed/cancelled and unavailable evidence. Progress is bounded and forecasts never claim admission. Focused real-store and actual-host tests pass, including failure/restart/repeated slots; no provider/live or rendered UI proof is inferred.

Automation management chat contract: `POST /v3/sessions` accepts the closed `purpose=automation_management` value only with Swarm and a resolved saved workspace; `handleSessionsV3PrimaryCreate` publishes protected `swarm_v3_session_purpose` / `swarm_v3_purpose_workspace_id` through canonical creation and existing idempotency hashing. `guardAutomationSessionMutation` prevents later purpose mutation. Sync shell and realtime membership preserve both fields. `POST /v3/sessions:discover` accepts `automation_management_workspace_id` with a recent selector, filtering the account/user-owned recent index before pagination; explicit session IDs cannot be combined with this filter. `desktop-automation-conversations.ts` and `automation-conversations.tsx` create only on a user gesture and reopen canonical sessions; `desktop-v3-session-visibility.ts` excludes them from ordinary navigation without dropping hydration. `ConfigureAutomationContext` supplies management guidance before a definition exists, never policy authority. Attack points are metadata forgery, cross-workspace discovery, replay creating duplicates and loss of purpose during reconnect. `sessions_v3_automation_management_test.go`, `session_purpose_test.go` and `desktop-automation-conversations.spec.ts` pass focused creation/replay/no-run, restart/account-workspace isolation and navigation-hydration checks. `automation_management_policy_test.go` proves management purpose adds no tool overlay in Plan or Auto, including `manage_automation`, while restricted occurrence policy still denies management/delegation tools. The existing mode/account/tool policy remains authoritative; live provider/reconnect behavior remains unproven.

Automation recovery authority: `runtime/automation.go:automationSweepAt` must reach recovery/outcome reconciliation even after a policy edit clears its grant. Missing current grants use store-owned definition attribution only for trusted recovery identity; domain current membership/session checks and Tick/Dispatch approval remain authoritative. `TestAutomationSweepRecoversWithoutCurrentGrant` executes real catalog traversal and checks zero paused Tick calls, recovery/outcome callbacks and unchanged definition. This is composition evidence, not connected provider execution or proof of every ownership-loss race.

Automation Desktop activity: `automation-overview.tsx` groups canonical occurrence heads by `scheduled_at` in the chosen display timezone and shows recorded state counts. `automation-workspace.tsx` reads 50 occurrences through the existing scoped automation runtime; any incoming or outgoing cursor labels counts as incomplete page subtotals, including empty scan pages. `SearchAutomationRecords` scans 512 keys, is not date-ordered, and supplies no day-filtered snapshot total; the UI does not claim one. Loading, stale and failed reads remain explicit. Add automation creates a sidebar conversation directly without a draft form. `automation-workspace.tsx` keeps one `AutomationConversations` sidebar across overview/details/configuration and explicitly opens linked plan/occurrence sessions there. `automation-chat.tsx` embeds the existing V3 conversation pane, header, composer and mode controls without a separate draft interface. Creation defaults to ordinary Swarm Auto mode and retains mandatory worktree allocation and stable retry identity. `automation-summary.tsx` supplies readable saved definitions and management proposals; `automation-proposal.ts` validates nested display fields before rendering untrusted tool output. Continuous blocker watching, automatic unblocking and autonomous archival are not advertised as available automation triggers. Narrow layouts stack navigation/content/chat; rendered pixel quality remains unverified.

Automation shared-workflow source audit: ordinary chat and the Automations sidebar dispatch the same `AutomationProposalCard` and `desktopAutomations.mutate` into `/v3/automations`; `DesktopV3RuntimeProvider` retains the canonical stream for the unlocked shell, and `v3-realtime-controller` invalidates demanded automation pages on durable updates and reconnect. Historical transcript cards remain historical requests, not a second live definition authority. `AutomationEditor` now saves changed configurations paused with `approval_required` and no reused grant, preserving session identity and exact pins; fresh policy approval and enabling remain separate. Proposal acceptance state is keyed to complete parsed request bytes so replacement requests cannot inherit accepted state. Conversation discovery is bounded and explicitly refreshable; it is not a new transcript cache. `automation-workflow.browser.spec.ts` passes controlled-boundary creation/retry, workspace-filtered reopen, unmount isolation and separate proposal/enable acceptance checks; editor browser assertions, runtime shared-consumer/failure assertions and `automation/conversation_test.go` pin-preservation assertions remain unexecuted in this change. Full browser/live reconnect proof remains outstanding. Event intake is local approving-user/exact-policy admission, not external webhooks or automatic session monitoring. Archive/integration still need separate approval; continuous supervision and automatic unblocking are not implemented by this page.

Automation Desktop consumers: `PolicyPanel` reviews the saved definition revision/digest, approves then links by separate CAS, exposes partial-link failure, and revokes with the independent grant revision. Enabling remains separate. `OutcomeSession` resolves typed `outcome.occurrence_id` through scoped get before opening canonical conversation/artifact inspection; free-text facts are not artifact references or session authority. Linked plan chat carries automation context without granting execution or automatically applying management proposals. Updates group only the loaded bounded search page, not a complete daily digest. No delete API, dedicated management-session creation or rich outcome-card deliverable index is provided; browser usability, live scheduling/restart, socket authentication and provider execution remain unvalidated.

### Automation conversion and progress validation

Typed `SessionPlanDocument.automation` references an exact paused definition. `validatePlanAutomation` accepts interval elapsed seconds (60..31622400) without cron-only timezone; cron retains its explicit timezone. Ordinary plan acceptance rejects typed recurrence. `PolicyApproval.ApproveUser` and `permission.CoordinateAutomationAcceptance` coordinate `CreateAutomationApprovalWithSession`: exact proposal digest/revision, grant, session binding and cancellation of the pending review permission commit together. Approval leaves the proposal inactive; enabling is a separate CAS. `ReviewPlanAutomation` supplies a nonempty proposal mutation identity; restricted sidechat edits remain non-applied. The active-plan API supplies canonical document digests; clients must not hash another serialization.

`ScheduleProgress` reuses `Due` over bounded revision/day windows and occurrence heads (50 definition revisions, eight pages of 50 occurrences). It exposes completeness, interval anchor, civil-day UTC bounds, distinct manual/unknown triggers and conditional future slots. It does not turn forecasts into a success denominator. Actual start/completion and unrecorded missed totals remain unavailable. `TickAt` admits durable due receipts; `RecoverPage` dispatches pending work; `AutomationExecutionHost.Start` persists/wakes canonical intents; `ReconcileOutcome` reads canonical checkpoint evidence. Newly allocated legacy occurrence sessions now carry trusted `WorktreeAdmissionEvidence` from the actual allocation. Pending old-revision work retains its pin but cannot dispatch after a definition edit; pause is not cancellation of already running work.

Executed on `f8e997d245b318b45a4696afa07026df3bafa784` plus scoped validation corrections: focused eight-package Go selection repeated twice; 13 Desktop state/component/structural tests; TypeScript. New `scheduled_lifecycle_test.go` proves exact 1/day, 4/day and 24 interval due slots, replay deduplication, exact instruction pins, edit rejection and reapproval using real approval/store/scheduler with a recording runtime. Conversion transaction success/replay and stale no-partial-grant assertions execute real V3 store mutations. Browser fixtures exercise actual controls but mocked APIs; no provider execution, live timing tolerance, full-shell pixel inspection or end-to-end HTTP conversion certification is implied. Independent audit remains required.

### Consumer verification rule

Before deleting or changing a route, search exact route constants/strings in `web/src`, `internal/app`, `internal/client`, scripts, and tests. Registration proves exposure; it does not prove a current production consumer. In particular, the checked-in sync contract itself says legacy workset removal requires static guards, runtime logs, cursor/snapshot/outbox tests, and live cross-client E2E (`V3SyncWorksetRemovalGates`).

Committed-child correction adds no HTTP route. The registered `task` schema/regular launch parser and `manage-worktree recall/help` are its model-facing clients; existing V3 session metadata mutation, delegated generation/owner records and read-only program inspection remain the durable authorities. See §6 and the committed-child row in §10 for trust boundaries and executed assertions.

## 9. Operational and release gates

- `scripts/check-precommit.sh`: formatting/static policy, path/secret/privacy/dependency checks, plus `scripts/check-atlas-sync.sh`; the legacy broad Go test path remains opt-in (`README.md:201-208`; script source).
- `scripts/run-critical-tests.sh {fast,deep,agents,all}`: the explicit atlas-driven executable manifest. `fast` covers deterministic listener, authentication/vault, authority, containment, media/image/video, FFmpeg-input, workspace-aware background `/task` admission, TUI/client routing, and critical Desktop V3 state/realtime/task-routing failures; `deep` covers Pebble mutation/outbox/replay/restart/concurrency, executor idempotency, delegated Git integration, and artifact Git integrity; `agents` covers system-agent identity/tool authority, launch trust rejection, Task Program and Iteration Swarm scheduling, subagent capacity/approval, durable child lineage/recovery, and Desktop child/realtime presentation. Every invocation is package/function-bounded, verifies each declared Go package/pattern resolves at least one test before execution, and uses explicit Go/Node test timeouts.
- `scripts/check-hardcoded-paths.sh` permits the reviewed Chrome package only in the exact renderer constant and exact readonly testbench declaration; it does not exempt the wrapper file or installation directory. `tests/scripts/hardcoded_chrome_path_test.py` executes the guard against isolated positive and ten negative fixtures (alternate paths, appended commands, changed declaration and unrelated filenames), asserting rejection and unchanged source. Wrapper argument forwarding remains checked by `scripts/test-testbench-container-routing.sh`; this is guard evidence, not live browser validation.
- `scripts/check-daemon-storage-paths.sh` classifies only the exact `ArtifactV3AuthorService.Rename` call between validated project-relative paths under `state.root` as a reviewed non-migration operation. It does not exempt the author file: other renames, migration helpers, home/XDG and temp/workspace defaults remain scanned. `tests/scripts/artifact_rename_storage_guard_test.py` runs the real guard on the current author and twelve negative fixtures with unchanged-source assertions. Storage self-test output is isolated under its `mktemp` directory (honoring `TMPDIR`), not a shared fixed output file. This is lexical guard evidence, not a new containment guarantee.
- `scripts/check-prepush.sh`: pre-push gate for protected branches; runs precommit plus the critical `fast` suite and must not be bypassed. Its pnpm advisory query uses three individually time-bounded attempts, retries only responses that do not contain a complete audit report, and still fails immediately on reported vulnerabilities or after registry unavailability exhausts the bounded attempts.
- `.github/workflows/critical-tests.yml`: checks out the declared PR/push head, installs the required repository-policy search tool, fetches the exact declared comparison commits, then runs repository policy/secret/path/atlas checks plus all three critical tiers on `dev`/`main` pull requests and `dev` pushes. `.github/workflows/install-distro-smoke.yml` builds one exact candidate and verifies clean Ubuntu and Arch systemd installs from curl availability through active service, CLI invocation, unset `TMPDIR`, and canonical runtime ownership. `.github/workflows/dependency-vulnerability-scan.yml` independently runs Go/web dependency scanners on PRs, dev pushes, and a schedule (aligned with critical-tests and install-distro-smoke to omit push-to-main where PR qualification already verified dependencies); concurrency is event-scoped as well as exact-SHA-scoped so a pull-request scan cannot cancel the required push scan for the same candidate, while duplicate runs of one event type still supersede older in-progress work. Current dependency evidence requires the `golang.org/x/crypto` fix for CVE-2026-56854. `.github/workflows/build-main.yml` consumes exact merged-source GCP qualification through `scripts/gcp-release-input.py` without requiring retired Astra schema policies or failing on unset `GCP_RELEASE_POLICY_JSON`; GitHub performs no build/test/package work. Keyless signing and the custom `https://swarm.dev/attestations/release-promotion/v1` attestation identify GitHub as promoter, not builder. `verify-release-evidence.sh` verifies that typed signed predicate, evidence digest, canonical GCP input digest, actual archive and embedded source/version metadata before upload and again behind `stable-release` approval. Legacy SLSA verification requires explicit `--legacy-slsa`; no failed-verification fallback exists.
- `scripts/check-launch-readiness.sh --require-clean`: composes launch defaults with cleanliness/public-repo checks (`scripts/check-launch-readiness.sh`).
- `scripts/testbench-container-deploy.sh`, `scripts/lib-testbench-e2e.sh`, and `docs/testing/testbench-container.md`: maintained candidate deployment uses a two-slot broker-owned `systemd-nspawn` pool. The deployment client exposes `deploy/status/stop/tunnel/pool-status`; `scripts/testbench-e2e-tunnel.sh` owns `check/run`, exact-HEAD checking, conditional deployment and temporary forwards. Slot-specific ports are derived from the assigned lane; input configuration validates slot-1 defaults and supports `SWARM_TESTBENCH_ENV_FILE`. Both wrapper actions reject occupied local ports. Existing user-specified URLs must be inspected directly through authenticated clients; configuration or forward-port failures do not justify replacing the endpoint. Container slots do not limit suite counts: the launch pre-run has separate bounded concurrency. These source contracts are not proof of live pool ownership, candidate identity, or successful concurrent execution.
- `scripts/run-testbench-launch-prerun.sh`: canonical bounded launch suite manifest for the local deterministic `critical` gate plus onboarding, Desktop, TUI, plan-auto, task routing, Task Programs, provider sync/realtime, and an explicit opt-in `omarchy-install` checksum-bound exact-archive lane (`--list-suites` is the manifest authority). Omarchy has no official OCI image: its lane targets a clean throwaway VM overlay produced by the official ISO's unattended `cidata` path, while `scripts/test-install-distro.sh` owns automated official-image Ubuntu/Arch coverage by downloading the same archive/checksum pair into a new user's home before installation. Ubuntu image bootstrap uses `scripts/install-distro-ubuntu-bootstrap.sh`: official image repositories/trust remain unchanged, APT acquisition has zero retries and 30-second HTTP/HTTPS timeouts, incomplete index updates fail closed, and update/install have 180/240-second total deadlines with 15-second progress messages. These disposable-image settings also apply to subsequent installer-owned Git acquisition; they are not host installer defaults. `tests/scripts/install_distro_bootstrap_test.py` executes fake APT/runtime failure paths and real accelerated deadlines; it does not establish live mirror health. The `task-routing` lane exercises Auto and Plan from the current workspace, a configured second saved workspace, and an existing-session context; each result must retain the selected binding/source, create a distinct session-owned worktree, publish its durable user message/run intent, and then stop any still-active bounded probe. Provider usage is recorded when it arrives, while provider-response completion remains covered by the separate provider-sync lane. The critical lane runs from the candidate checkout; the other lanes exercise the configured loopback-only testbench. Non-secret `.env` role assignments are Fireworks-only and pin the bounded live proof posture independently for Auto, Plan, Coder, and Designer so execution never depends on mutable catalog recommendations or a Codex fallback. `scripts/render-testbench-model-config.sh` renders that exact posture for installation as the root-owned testbench release-gate configuration; local wrappers and automatic/focused remote gate cycles therefore use the same validated Action/Plan model IDs and thinking levels. Fireworks assignments omit `service_tier` so the catalog's default standard path applies; runners must not reuse Codex's `fast` tier. Runtime usage assertions normalize only Fireworks's provider response/request path to its final catalog-ID segment while retaining exact provider and run lineage checks.
- Attach-only is a separate explicit launch-prerun path: `--attach-only` plus reviewed suite selection skips `.env` and deployment entirely. `critical` (compiler fan-out capped at two), read-only `attach-inspect`, and local `workspace-safety`/`workspace-browser` fixtures are admitted; `workspace-routing` requires an explicit reviewed disposable fixture parent and a bounded 25-second supervisor cleanup grace; registered `workspace-workers` remains disabled pending owned-child cancellation and exact task-permission handling; legacy live suites above mutate shared models/workspaces or ambient sessions and fail closed in this mode. `testbench-attach.mjs:AttachClient` uses the exact loopback Desktop origin, bounded same-origin bootstrap/topology/settings reads, redirect denial, 15-second maximum requests, 1 MiB response cap, identity pinning and settings-drift rejection. Broker build/lane evidence remains separate; HTTP status cannot establish it. `lib-launch-prerun.sh` passes structured argv to `launch-prerun-supervisor.py:run`: bounded suite overlap, 600-second wall cap, stall/heartbeat/output limits, Linux subreaper/process-identity cancellation and atomic per-suite results. Fresh private evidence is preserved, never overwritten. These are runner boundaries, not a sandbox or remote-provider cancellation authority. Matrix L1 defines 48 product subcases; their missing executable proofs remain later work.
- `docs/main-deploy-checklist.md` and release workflows are the publication authority when present at the target revision.

These gates are commands, not timeless claims that the repository passes. Revision 4 records the exact focused executions performed when this manifest was introduced; later revisions must record their own evidence.

### Runner-only dedicated Codex bridge

`swarmd/cmd/testbench-codex-broker` is an explicit test executable, not a daemon API. Private per-lane UNIX listeners expose only `GET /ready` and `POST /response`; `internal/testbenchcodex.Broker.Handler` bounds/validates requests and serializes provider execution. The broker alone owns a new dedicated credential store and existing Codex device login/refresh implementation; candidates receive response data, never tokens. `scripts/testbench-codex-overlay.py` opts candidate builds into the bridge registry and canonical model configuration; ordinary runtime registration remains unchanged. `scripts/local_testbench_codex.py` extends the local pool without changing its running supervisor, binds only one lane socket and requires exact HEAD/generation/profile for attachment. `lib-testbench-e2e.sh` accepts explicit local Codex Luna/medium while preserving remote container/Fireworks behavior. See `scripts/local-testbench-codex.md` for operations and live-proof gaps.

## 10. Security- and durability-critical matrix

Retained artifact tool boundary: `Runtime.Definitions` → `executeManageArtifact` → `importManagedArtifact` distinguishes native/legacy exact references and derives destination identity. `readDirectArtifactV3HTML` separately authenticates the destination caller and delegates retained reads; it does not grant source writes. `ReadArtifactV3PreviewEvidence` checks retained destination authority and evidence digest, while API/Desktop expose inherited provenance without a fabricated new render. Attacks include forged ownership, mixed/null refs, malformed importer projections advertised as ready, read-only import, source selection drift, dropped empty-page cursors, and lost or rebound preview evidence. `runtime_manage_artifact_retained_test.go` asserts zero pre-authority calls on malformed requests and no ready response on incomplete projection; `artifact_v3_retained_reuse_test.go` authors real-store rejection/no destination repository, source immutability, inherited evidence corruption/deletion and pending-only Studio checks. Codex schema and Desktop normalization tests cover actual consumer transformations. All new integration tests remain unexecuted until an approved leased environment exists; no live or pixel proof.

Retained artifact import foundation: principal isolation, exact reference discrimination, source immutability, bounded discovery, exact tree/evidence provenance and atomic independent publication are owned by `ArtifactV3Service.Import/ResolveRetainedSource`, `artifact_v3_import_receipt.go`, `Authority.Import`, `prepareV3ArtifactMutation` and `prepareAuthoritativeArtifactParts`. Attack points include mixed references, request reuse with changed sources, corrupt objects, metadata-only ready claims, source-chain adoption, scan truncation and Git/Pebble crash windows. Requirement-first `artifact_v3_import_atomic_test.go`, `artifact_v3_reuse_test.go` and `authority_import_test.go` target those boundaries; focused tests passed twice after disk capacity recovery and repair of a read-only-object fixture, a wrong failure hook, an incomplete profile fixture and a pre-existing obsolete unknown-base CAS assertion. The latter now asserts integrity rejection plus no mutation, matching exact-ready historical-base admission. No critical-runner promotion, installed-runtime availability, live provider or pixel/render proof is claimed. The later tool/runtime integration is source-reviewed only; its execution evidence is explicitly separate.

Successor admission: `run.adoptSessionWorktree` preserves dirty prior and same-owner reselected lanes, and allocates same-source successors (exact saved ID/generation/path). Allocation uses the saved checkout's configured current/base branch commit, never copies dirty bytes or implicitly carries active-lane commits. V3 CAS publishes old/new `swarm_v3_worktree_history` identities together; rejection rolls back newly allocated resources. `validateSessionRepositoryIdentity` checks history base/source/generation as well as branch/owner. Same-source adoption preserves retained repository descendants and program destinations; attachment changes remain stricter. `programWorkspacePath` and `repositoryLane` authenticate and reuse the persisted lane before consulting the parent's new default. `session.EnsureWorkspaceTransitionIdle` rejects declared/running repository programs without reconciliation, while managed-only Designer programs do not pin adoption. `SessionStore.CreateTaskProgram` shares the session mutation lock with canonical adoption, which rechecks active repository programs before publication; idempotent replay remains before this guard. High-value attacks: stale source/history, foreign retained lane, allocation collision, CAS failure and scheduling before first allocation. `TestManageWorkspaceAdoptWorktreePreservesDirtySuccessor` and `TestWorkspaceSuccessor*` exercise these admission/preservation boundaries; `TestTaskProgramPersistedLaneSurvivesSuccessor` and the real-stage regression prove old-lane contents/base, later-stage integration and dirty refusal; the adoption guard test injects scheduling between preflight and mutation. The joined adoption regression now creates a terminal program before two canonical successor mutations and verifies its immutable destination plus exact durable inventory for all three parent lanes. Multi-workspace tests distinguish revocation of an additional attachment (remove its runtime roots, retain primary execution) from primary revocation (reject). These hermetic checks do not prove provider-backed concurrent scheduling or installed runtime delivery.

Recovery safety: `manage_workspace cancel_worktree_recovery` is exact-claimant, revision-guarded and permission-gated cancellation through V3, never filesystem cleanup or a session switch. `run/service_workspace_recovery_action.go` journals copy destination ownership before `worktree.CopyRecoveryJournaled` creates resources; `store/pebble/session_worktree_recovery.go` reserves both lanes and atomically publishes or retains the destination on cancellation. `task_program_store.go` serializes active program admission with the ownership fence. Recovery discovery exposes interrupted-operation metadata without reading reserved content. Negative tests cover journal failure, external destination occupation, store reopen and rejected program admission; arbitrary host writers and orphan subprocesses remain outside the demonstrated fence.

Runtime delivery: `manage_workspace` with `action=adopt_worktree` and a short `worktree_name` allocates a successor; optional `expected_worktree_path` guards stale selection. An exact owned historical `worktree_path` instead requests reselection and preserves dirty source/target bytes under durable admission. Successful output requests a provider-turn restart so subsequent tool scope uses the canonical new identity. Source changes require a separately authorized scoped commit/integration, exact-head build gates and approved runtime delivery before this behavior is available in an installed daemon/Desktop. Revalidate adoption, retained dirty bytes/program targets and sidebar allocation/reconnect on an isolated candidate; do not restart the host service or mutate historical production sessions as a substitute.

2026-09-10 source-audit revision: daemon `ConfigureAutomations` now installs `automation/approved_events.go` in both API verification and execution admission. Local event intake derives authority from the authenticated approving user, exact current enabled definition/source, canonical plan pins and unexpired/unrevoked durable approval (`PolicyApproval.Verify`); it is not an external webhook credential surface. `approved_events_test.go` asserts foreign-user/stale/source rejection without occurrences, replay identity and revocation. Not run; parent validation required. Connected follow-up: `runtime/automation.go:automationOutcomes` reads canonical session/plan evidence via `ExecutionService.ReconcileOutcome`, then independently calls `notification.AutomationDeliveryService`; failures do not dispatch execution. Terminal reservations are reclaimed by `ClaimAutomationDispatch` only after terminal occurrence evidence. Cancellation admits an exact revision into `cancelling` before the host fence/stop and publishes `cancelled` only after success. Source audit is not end-to-end execution proof.

Automation foundation (now composed by `runtime/daemon.go`): `store/pebble/automation_store.go` owns account/workspace-encoded records, immutable attributed revisions, synced CAS/idempotency batches and trigger uniqueness. `automation/service.go` validates 1–16 ordered canonical plan bindings and earlier-binding dependencies through `SessionStore.GetPlanRevision`, pins document SHA-256, checks ownership/approval through injected Access, and separates user locks from occurrence-attributed untrusted summaries. `History` is bounded revision retrieval; search scans at most 512 keys per page with scope/filter-bound opaque continuations. Likely attack points: forged principals, stale writes, same-version plan replacement, dependency ambiguity, receipt replay, context permission injection and cross-scope cursors. Authored real-store and fake-domain assertions cover rejected writes/grants, restart replay, commit failure, namespace ambiguity, locked context, history and DST folds/gaps; none executed. Full-document pins deliberately fail closed on canonical lifecycle document changes. The daemon now composes Access/approval, canonical V3 execution host, API/tool consumers, scheduler recovery and notification delivery; no parallel checkpoint executor is introduced.

Workspace catalog consistency invariant: failed/stale/foreign mutations cannot emit a catalog change or partially rewrite its indexes. Catalog changes and their V3 outbox row share one batch; signals contain no paths/content and account admission precedes delivery. Threats: cross-account control-frame leakage, stale detail responses resurrecting deletions, and per-component burst amplification. `workspace_catalog_realtime_test.go` in store and API tests rejection/no-change, restart, socket delivery/replay and cursor validation; client `workspace-catalog-refresh.spec.ts` and `workspace-catalog-effects.spec.ts` test coalescing, trailing invalidation, errors, replay and retained Git details. One shared cache coordinator is per tab; separate tabs still perform bounded lightweight hydration per event, not polling. Live multi-tab load/visual proof and independent test review remain open.

Repository inventory invariant: retained provenance cannot become an execution or mutation grant. Likely attack points are cross-principal cursors, source/lane substitution, stale catalog generations, symlink/branch replacement, historical commit targeting and unbounded retained-child scans. Authority is canonical session/store claims plus current workspace catalog and managed Git ownership. Negative real-Git HTTP tests assert foreign/stale/mismatched rejection and unchanged HEAD, staging and dirty bytes; store tests cover authenticated cursors, parent-scoped invalidation, restart/backfill and exact late-lane lookup. UI operations are disabled for stale or non-current targets and selection is not silently replaced by default changes. No test was promoted into the critical runner.

## 11. Test topology and mapping guidance

Tests exist across multiple ecosystems: Go `_test.go` files in the root and `swarmd` modules, TypeScript/React `*.spec.*` files under `web`, shell/static harnesses under `tests` and `scripts`, and live E2E/testbench runners. Benchmarks and contract/static-source tests answer different questions from behavior tests. `docs/testing/test-audit-ledger.tsv` is the two-pass classification authority for the wider inherited corpus; `scripts/run-critical-tests.sh` is the smaller executable build-blocking manifest selected from inspected assertions.

For a critical behavior, build a **requirement-first map**:

1. state the invariant and threat/failure mode;
2. cite the production registration, boundary, service, and store symbols;
3. list direct tests, contract/static guards, integration tests, and live gates separately;
4. inspect assertions and fixtures—not names—to record what each test actually proves;
5. record missing negative, concurrency, restart, cross-account, traversal, and failure-injection cases;
6. run the narrow package/function or the appropriate checked-in `fast`/`deep`/`agents` manifest; never substitute an unbounded module-wide run.

Never use test count as coverage. Never infer “covered,” “secure,” or “passing” from file presence. A route-to-test entry in §8 means “start inspection here.”

### Critical-suite growth rule

Promote tests into `scripts/run-critical-tests.sh` one invariant at a time. Revision 6 promoted existing independently reviewed listener, media cleanup/isolation, image validation/path, source-media traversal/staleness, render ownership, and FFmpeg drawtext tests, and added direct locked-vault middleware rejection. The next high-value gaps are Workspace Action execution lifecycle/TOCTOU, vault interruption and runtime panic redaction, Pebble commit and outbox-crash failure injection, account-isolated developer diagnostics, hermetic HTML renderer network/file escape, media multipart concurrency, deeper FFmpeg filter/control-character confinement, and a hermetic full Task Program executor simulation spanning child launch → committed handoff → parent integration → dependent-stage unlock. Each addition requires inspected assertions, a negative or injected-failure case, a bounded timeout, successful repeated focused execution, an atlas §10 update, and first-/second-pass evidence in `docs/testing/test-audit-ledger.tsv`. Tests that encode retired contracts stay outside the build gate until corrected; do not weaken current production contracts to make inherited tests pass.

## 12. Known uncertainties and fact-check notes

- Shared-capacity validation is local Go only. New regression fixtures exercise real V3 mutations and fake providers, including a 501-record head-blocked paging setup (not 501 simultaneously running models). Existing `TestSessionsV3ExecutorFailsStaleRunningRunAfterRestartWithoutResume` and `TestSessionsV3ExecutorRecoversPendingRunAfterRestart` fail during obsolete mutable-Swarm fixture setup; the inherited overflow-compaction test fails on unmatched configured provider before reaching compaction. These are not claimed as passing; canonical capacity/restart/direct-compaction regressions are separate. Repair inherited fixtures in the final validation checkpoint. No critical-manifest promotion or independent two-pass test-audit approval is claimed. Settings UI is source-inspected, not rendered or executed.

- **Multi-workspace core repair proved with qualified live acceptance, not launch approval:** exact attachment/default CAS, actual repository/branch/base validation, retained history, stale-invoker/batch barriers, worker source identity and bounded committed dependency evidence now have final-source repeated regression results (matrix V6). HTTP creation persists allocator base and rejects forged base before allocation. An exact-build Finder → two overlapping disjoint Coders → Designer trial completed; parent validation caught incorrect fixture output, and exact-lineage visual repair corrected clipped headings. The original outputs remain retained, candidate selection stays explicit, and later fixture correction was not a fresh Designer dependency run. Full-app attachment/default, retained dirty file pointer access, restart/reload and unsupported-plan rejection are observed. Retained worker history conservatively pins default/removal, even when completed; contradictory or missing legacy provenance rejects without guessing or deleting work. Still unverified: regular cross-repository provider integrations, held-worker/default races, crash/TOCTOU windows, mid-stage permission denial, complete full-app status/transport and large-set timing matrices, and independent P1/P2 test review. Native reference discovery/fresh-context retention also exposed a bounded product gap; canonical exact-reference notes enabled recovery, not a discovery fix. Isolated-candidate deployment evidence is separate from host/release promotion and current-health claims.

- Desktop request lifecycle source now opts auth bootstrap, origin/onboarding/vault status and V3 bootstrap/hydrate into 15s budgets; Git status uses 15s and its 25s server long poll uses 35s. `apiFetch` streams and unrelated mutations retain no new global deadline. Cancellation detaches auth waiters without cancelling other users of the bounded shared bootstrap. Git Query observers pass their owned signals; page hide/pagehide abort active reads. The Desktop session Git long-poll consumer has since been removed in favor of durable inventory updates; the retained endpoint is not a freshness fallback. The unused HTML session preload had no consumer and was removed rather than kept as a second startup read. Pre-app recovery is now source-implemented with a 20s inline watchdog, stylesheet render-block release and explicit committed-screen handoff. Recovery is available only after HTML delivery with a responsive renderer and permitted inline script (background timer throttling can delay it); neither the incident root cause nor installed-runtime health is established. Chromium failure injection verifies both the HTML/React component boundary and production-built main/router/admission paths against synthetic HTTP responses; healthy means the legitimate locked-vault gate, not an unlocked daemon-backed session. Git navigation uses real request APIs and Query/poll owners in a component fixture, not the full workspace UI. These checks do not prove installed application behavior, browser service-worker upgrade timing, or non-Chromium engines. React lazy routes replace TanStack's automatic missing-module reload; unused route prefetch is removed so a failed speculative chunk cannot hide admission/onboarding errors. Service-worker controller changes no longer reload, navigation is not intercepted, and explicit page reload never unregisters workers or clears caches/auth/storage. The inherited `review-worktrees-modal.spec.ts` cannot load because it imports undeclared `vitest`; this optional source-wiring suite was not treated as passing.

- Review Manage now displays navigation before status/patch checks and serializes abortable background refreshes. Integration scopes reads to selected lanes and does not await unrelated UI refetches. Cold catalog still resolves repository inventory and searches up to 200 owned review sessions; full classification still waits for its slowest comparison. Actual click-to-card and integration latency, browser rendering, and new regressions remain unverified; no near-instant timing claim.

1. `sessions_v3_sync_contract.go` still calls bootstrap/hydrate/stream “future canonical,” while routes and clients are implemented and the repository contract calls them canonical. Treat the routes as current implementation and the wording as stale until intentionally reconciled.
2. `/ws`, `/v1/sessions*`, v2 agent/custom-tool routes, and V3 workset/TUI routes are registered, but registration alone does not identify all active consumers. Follow the consumer verification rule before removal.
3. The provider registry includes OpenAI in addition to the README’s named support list; Copilot is deliberately dormant. Use runtime registration, not README or directory names, for availability.
4. This atlas statically inspected representative handlers, registrations, contracts, authorities, and tests. It did not exhaustively prove each handler’s accepted HTTP methods or every client caller. Handler-level method tables should be generated only from inspected switch logic, not guessed from REST naming.
5. Atlas revisions 1–3 ran no tests and asserted no runtime health. Revision 4 introduced and executed bounded critical manifests; that evidence applies only to the named manifest at that working revision, not to the wider repository test corpus.
6. `dev_mode` session dump is an operator/debug surface, not a tenant-isolated production read: at this revision it accepts an authenticated principal but looks up the requested session by ID without requiring matching `AccountScopeID`. Its authenticated download subpath serves only a private regular file produced in the dump directory by that flow. Keep development mode disabled outside disposable development/test environments and treat this as a security-review item.
7. Admitted Desktop/Tailscale origin persistence across daemon restart was not established strongly enough in this pass; inspect the origin store and restart path before documenting persistence semantics.
8. Video rendering executes local host FFmpeg rather than a retired container sandbox. Its security therefore depends on opaque registered source references, path validation, bounded invocation, and process controls; no stronger OS-isolation claim is made.
9. Mint/activation reporting and authenticated feedback are current implementation and have focused transport/state/startup tests, but the inherited two-pass ledger inventory predates the feedback test file. The workspace-aware `/task` change also materially edited and added backend, TUI, Desktop, and live-runner test symbols after that inventory; affected inherited rows remain `stale-inventory`. Revision 15 source-bound the corrected live runner and the new focused parser rows with independent first/second-pass `keep` verdicts, while the other materially edited inherited task rows still require the maintained full-corpus reinventory workflow. Focused passing evidence does not make the wider audit complete.
10. Initial critical-suite execution exposed inherited tests that encode stale contracts (including mutable system-agent profiles, non-loopback startup, agent creation without a tool contract, legacy empty-user sessions, and routed starts without `agent_name`). The agent-suite audit additionally found stale prose-literal assertions in `runtime_task_contract_test.go` and `service_task_swarm_test.go`, repaired a positive-auth fixture that omitted the now-required tool contract, and exposed obsolete Desktop startup-profile expectations, an extensionless ESM import failure in `desktop-chat-panel.badge.spec.ts`, and independently unstable/failing full `v3-realtime-controller.spec.ts` cases. These remain outside the build-blocking manifests pending two-pass correction; production contracts were not weakened to admit them.
11. The deterministic `fast` tier now gates workspace-aware `/task` admission and client routing while `agents` proves the wider delegation contracts without external providers. The checked-in testbench has provider-backed `task-routing` and `task-program` suites. Those live suites require the configured loopback SSH testbench and are not CI/build gates; passing unit/integration tiers must not be represented as proof of a real provider successfully launching agents.
12. `panic_privacy_test.go` is intentionally a static forbidden-sink guard. It prevents reintroducing obvious stack/panic formatting in two execution paths but does not prove runtime response/log redaction; a dynamic panic-injection test remains required.
13. The pre-merge audit promoted existing deterministic protection for registered media roots and FFmpeg drawtext escaping, but host FFmpeg still has no stronger process sandbox guarantee and HTML capture still lacks hermetic request-interception tests.

14. Desktop realtime evidence limitation: delayed-close timer loss is deterministically reproduced and corrected in source. This does not establish the cause of an origin-specific browser freeze. Incident-time sanitized HTTP request timing/occupancy (separate from upgraded WebSockets), socket event ordering, running build identity and, if responses complete while UI stalls, a bounded main-thread trace are still needed. No deployment or restart is implied by the focused tests.

15. Manifest repair audit: six focused deterministic regressions passed twice in the parent checkout; changed test-file digests and symbol inventory are refreshed, without claiming independent P1/P2 ledger approval. A real provider Designer retry on the repaired deployed runtime and inspection of its rendered animation are separate requirements; fake renderer bytes prove no visual result. Existing unrelated V2-era atlas sections remain historical reconciliation debt, not new managed-write authority.

### Independent fact-check performed for this revision

The first fact-check used four independent adversarial scopes (API, security, runtime/orchestration, and product boundaries). This subsequent audit used five fresh scopes: API/middleware, V3 durability/runtime, workspace/execution security, artifacts/media, and product/client/revision integrity. Parent reconciliation confirmed the core architecture and exposed additional concrete errors: missing `/v1` and `/v1/workspace` route prefixes, an over-broad vault-gate summary, a nonexistent Action test path, imprecise V3 collection/nested-dispatch attribution, and system-agent/tool-contract citation gaps. Conflicting or weak claims were resolved in favor of registration/implementation evidence or retained as explicit unknowns. Unsupported specifics (notably exhaustive HTTP methods, universal consumers, passing coverage, admitted-origin persistence, and container-like video isolation) remain omitted or unresolved.

## 13. Glossary

- **Attach token:** daemon access token accepted by authenticated local API middleware.
- **Artifact (current V1):** immutable, lineage-bearing managed output stored under session authority; not synonymous with a workspace file.
- **Working Artifact (Artifact V2):** implemented durable authoring aggregate whose real Parts, immutable Part Revisions, Composition Head, Build/Validation Results, Iteration Rounds, and Published Head remain separate first-class objects. Managed Designer authoring uses a context-bound V2 capability, and Desktop now reads the dedicated `/v3/sessions/{session_id}/artifact-v2` catalog/Studio projection for immediate working visibility, real part history, diagnostics, candidates, exact selection, and locks.
- **Checkpoint / attempt:** durable unit and execution attempt inside a session plan.
- **Endpoint cursor:** opaque signed position within one exact sync scope; not a client-comparable integer.
- **Execution epoch:** bounded provider-context interval within a durable session.
- **Live patch:** ephemeral streaming text/reasoning acceleration; durable messages/events remain authority.
- **Outbox:** Pebble-backed ordered records used for durable replay and realtime fanout.
- **Principal:** authenticated user/account authority injected into request/runtime context.
- **Projection:** current durable session view derived and committed with events.
- **Run intent:** durable record of requested/running/terminal execution used for recovery.
- **System agent:** immutable code-owned identity and tool/security contract.
- **Target:** generic Swarm routing/topology destination; not a retired container profile.
- **Workspace Action:** account-owned, workspace-scoped structured executable definition; definition management is not execution.
- **Worktree:** isolated Git checkout/branch used for a session or delegated Coder scope.

### Native preview contract diagnostics (2026-09-08)

`htmlcapture.animationSeekAckValidator` is shared by bootstrap echo bookkeeping and conversion frame capture: exact finite `time_ms`, optional nonempty string `scene_id`, no unknown fields. Integer frame timestamps remain floor(index*1000/FPS), including the final frame strictly before duration; no rounding tolerance or fabricated acknowledgement is introduced. `TestAnimationSeekSceneContract` exercises 0/500/966ms samples at 30 FPS, rejects re-quantized timestamps and malformed metadata, and asserts no returned deliverable media on failure. Conversion does not infer scene identity from a string; native preview retains expected-scene validation.

`artifactV3PreviewCaptureRequest` retains strict animation API version/functions, ready timing and seek time/scene checks. It emits fixed failure codes for incompatible APIs, rejected ready/seek calls and mismatched acknowledgements. `htmlcapture.captureState` passes only these six allowlisted codes through its selection catch; `safeMessage` supplies actionable text without forwarding authored exceptions. Likely failure/attack points are omitted API version, incorrect scene acknowledgements and private exception text. No new route, runtime fallback or weakening of installed Three.js graph integrity is introduced.

`TestArtifactV3PreviewDiagnosticBridge` executes the injected bridge in Node against missing/wrong version, missing seek, ready/seek rejection, duration/fps/time/scene mismatch, and successful pause/sample/acknowledgement fixtures, asserting no selected-state update on rejection and unchanged source bytes. `TestCaptureSelectionDiagnostics` executes the renderer catch against allowlisted, unknown and non-Error exceptions. Both passed twice alongside existing scene and offline-runtime integrity tests on the scoped diff based on `0c11b299`. This is executable JavaScript contract evidence, not browser initialization, GPU, original-artifact recovery or visual-quality proof. Independent audit remains pending; tests are not promoted to the curated runner.

### Native temporal scene authority (2026-09-08)

Native `ArtifactV3Part.temporal` carries stable `scene_id` (equal to Part ID), inclusive start/exclusive end, and an optional in-interval sample; otherwise `PreviewTimeMS` chooses the midpoint. `ValidateArtifactV3Scenes` requires ordered contiguous chapters covering the animation, permits shared real Canvas selectors and leaves legacy untimed/single-scene output valid. Explicit `scene_contract` on task/direct-create input is parsed before native allocation, persisted in the grant and inherited from exact source revisions; the producer cannot downgrade its required IDs/intervals. Initial authoring now shares `ReconcileParts`; focused revisions still cannot structurally replace Parts. Required policy remains immutable even for whole-project revisions; changing that policy is not silently authorized by reconciliation.

`artifactV3PreviewCaptureRequest` validates HTML duration, samples one shared playhead and requires scene-aware ready duration/fps plus exact seek time/scene acknowledgement. `Preview` records ordered Part/sample/PNG digests; `ArtifactV3Service.Create/SubmitCandidate` rejects missing, extra, stale-sample or foreign-Part evidence before Git mutation. Evidence is projected with the immutable revision and exposed by the existing native revision API, not a new route. Part projections retain intervals and derived capture time. Exact current-head target reuse goes through `PrepareArtifactV3Turn` ownership/head/projection/Part checks; historical read-only style-reference support is not newly introduced here. Direct create forwards the profile and required policy together, and same-run repair skips byte-identical locked manifests rather than attempting a forbidden no-op edit. The existing direct-create/temporal/capture-UI and production-path recovery regressions also passed twice.

Focused requirement-first tests: `artifact_v3_scenes_test.go` (store/runtime), `runtime_artifact_v3_scenes_test.go` (tool), `artifact_v3_scene_lineage_test.go` (runtime real Git/Pebble with fake capture), and `artifact_v3_scene_contract_test.go` (run parser). Executed named Scene/Temporal/Remix tests twice with GOMAXPROCS=2, -p 1 and 90-second package timeout. These prove contracts, evidence binding and unchanged state after stale/foreign/unknown reference rejection, not browser-executed acknowledgement, animation aesthetics or full playback. Existing outputs are not migrated; Desktop consumption is separate work. Independent test-audit passes and curated promotion remain pending.

### Native temporal HTML preview correction (2026-09-05)

Direct `manage_artifact create` accepts the closed `motion_ui` animation profile and stores its resolved snapshot in `swarm-artifact.json`. Explicit temporal Parts retain stable HTML selectors and a bounded midpoint `capture_time_ms`; static output selectors remain required in every capture. `htmlcapture.captureState` resolves retained required selectors before hiding explicitly marked capture UI with `display:none!important`; only selectors whose complete nonempty match set belongs to marked UI are exempt. Nodes stay addressable across states; missing/hidden output, unmarked navs and viewport clipping still reject. `runtime/artifact_v3_runtime.go:artifactV3PreviewCaptureRequest` checks the exact reviewed profile, samples each temporal selector through the authored `swarm.animation/v1` ready/seek runtime, and never forcibly reveals hidden scenes. `htmlcapture.Request.StateRequiredSelectors` adds per-state requirements; temporal capture pauses rather than cancels animations and retains network, blocking, viewport, containment and exact-pixel stability gates. Other direct HTML profiles remain explicitly unsupported. `tool/runtime_manage_artifact_html_parts.go:artifactHTMLCaptureOnlyRegions` excludes semantic start tags marked `data-swarm-capture-ui`; `createDirectArtifactV3HTML` rejects explicit capture-only IDs before turn allocation. `ArtifactHTMLAnimationDurationMS` validates the canonical timing manifest (shared 36000-frame budget at authored FPS). Automatic creation gives the first meaningful output region a whole-animation midpoint; other output regions remain global requirements. Profiled preview without temporal metadata synthesizes a deterministic midpoint state through the same ready/seek bridge, not live default capture. Source bytes and locked metadata are not rewritten during preview. No API route or auth boundary changes.

Critical invariant / attack points: profile-budget override, missing or out-of-range time, hidden/clipped required scene, unstable playhead, absent seek acknowledgement, incomplete state evidence. Focused tool/runtime contract tests and a real Chrome negative/stability test pass twice. This proves bounded contracts and browser fixtures, not provider repeatability or general visual reliability. Existing static/flexible-Part tests also pass twice. New tests are unreviewed for critical-tier promotion; the live six-scene trials remain a separate gate.

Current capture-UI/default-motion evidence (2026-09-07): focused derivation/create and runtime request tests pass on the scoped working diff; real local Chrome valid/hidden/clipped/unstable cases pass without a skip. New attack points are boolean/case/value attribute parsing, false marker text, duplicate/missing manifest, out-of-duration scene metadata, capture-only explicit selection and accidental wall-clock routing. Allocation tests assert no turn/submit/selection on rejection; request tests assert global output selectors and source immutability. Installed-runtime/retained-draft validation remains unperformed and requires explicit operator rebuild followed by explicit resume. Independent P1/P2 review and curated promotion remain pending.

### Theme tool saved-workspace targeting

`tool/runtime_manage_theme.go` resolves theme targets using the run's authenticated source workspace, independently of filesystem execution scope. Ordinary runtime dispatch obtains this from `run/service_workspace_context.go`; theme's dedicated inspection/approval dispatch (`executeManageThemeTool`, `buildManageThemePermissionPayload`) uses `buildPermissionWorkspaceScope` in `run/service_task_launch.go`, which must also preserve worktree/source metadata. Dropping that metadata advertises the execution lane even when the theme helper is correct. Set and create/update/batch application require an exact matched saved root before calling `workspace.Service.SetThemeIDForPrincipal`; ancestor matches and missing source identity fail before settings writes. Inspection advertises the same source default. Explicit account/global application remains separate. Attack point: treating an isolated lane under another saved workspace as a settings target. `runtime_manage_theme_workspace_test.go` authors preview/confirm and missing-source/ancestor/unsaved rejection postconditions; execution and independent review remain pending.

### Native exact-action permission rules

`permission.explainPolicyDecision` keeps native selection/rebinding default-ask and isolated from generic artifact/wildcard grants, but now honors an operator-configured exact `artifact_v3_select` or `artifact_v3_resume` rule. Exact denial precedes exact allowance. `runtime_manage_artifact_v3_native.selectDirectArtifactV3` still enforces owner and head/turn CAS; the tool schema and invalid-input error name mandatory `expected_turn_revision`. This fixes ignored action-specific consent, not ownership or CAS failures. `TestArtifactV3ExactActionApproval` and `TestArtifactV3ExactActionRules` plus focused native handoffs passed twice; live rebuilt-candidate verification remains pending.

### Native live Three.js serving clarification

`artifactV3RuntimeAdapter.OpenPreview` now provisions the same integrity-checked installed graph as capture through `artifactV3LiveRuntimeFiles`, but never injects capture pause/seek ownership. Entrypoint import maps and transitive module URLs retain the exact revision and preview capability. The existing `/v3/sessions/{id}/artifacts-v3/{id}/preview/access/{token}/files/...` handler grants noncredentialed CORS to opaque-origin JS only after capability validation; CSP, network restrictions and sandbox remain unchanged. Authored playback controls inside Parts bypass the selection capture listener. Native Studio uses authenticated live HTML, not the legacy `swarm-player/v1` seek driver; visibility/reduced-motion/WebGL handling remain authored responsibilities and closing/revising unmounts the frame. Attack points remain stale grants, substituted install bytes and transitive URL loss. New narrow runtime/API tests plus the selection browser test exercise these corrections; elapsed Three.js playback and original installed-screen behavior are separate, not established by these checks.

### Native viewer elapsed playback evidence

`desktop-v3-artifact-v3-playback.browser.spec.ts` mounts actual native Studio with the production injected selection script and API CSP, intercepted revision/capability endpoints and pinned local Three.js. Software-WebGL canvas PNG hashes change over elapsed time before any seek, stay stable while paused, and change after seek/resume. Assertions cover authored synthetic visibility handling, emulated reduced motion/manual play, real WebGL context loss, Close/reopen reset and module-fetch failure. Both opaque-origin module edges are requested with exact revision URLs. This is representative fixture evidence, not original-artifact access, full daemon/install integration, hardware GPU, real background-tab throttling or styled visual-quality proof. Native Studio does not supply these authored lifecycle/error handlers. Focused Go adapter/API tests independently cover preparation and capability rejection; the browser mock does not execute those Go paths.

### Native author marker-search cost

`runtime/artifact_v3_runtime.go:bytesContainsFold` serves Build document checks and `unresolvedArtifactV3Targets`. ASCII needles up to 64 bytes use early success without whole-source allocation and a total 256 inner-comparison budget; budget exhaustion, encountered non-ASCII source, non-ASCII needles and longer needles retain lowercase-substring semantics through the original matcher. The budget prevents repeated-prefix amplification. This is not a DOM validator or a substitute for browser evidence. The author service still authenticates each draft load, snapshots current files and invalidates readiness on edits; persistence and Finish authority are unchanged. Attack points are Unicode semantic drift, overlapping/partial markers, source mutation and successful-build registration after rejection. `artifact_v3_search_test.go` exercises differential matching and negative build postconditions; live-browser CPU attribution remains unmeasured.

### Fresh root candidate evidence (pending live validation)

`test-install-distro.sh --identity root` invokes `test-install-root-scenario.sh`
inside a disposable systemd distro, without pre-creating the service owner. It
uses the checksum-bound archive installer, checks actual MainPID and service-cgroup
UID/GID slots and loopback TCP listeners, writes a workspace once as the service
owner, and reinstalls without recreating the preservation probe. Root mode requires
rootless Podman with private cgroups; it refuses host-root containers and does not
mount the host cgroup tree. `tests/scripts/install_root_assertions_test.py` executes
the actual credential/listener predicates against synthetic positive and negative
kernel records (two tests passed); this does not prove live process behavior. `build-main.yml` requires this scenario
before signing. This is not yet executed distro evidence and does not prove
authenticated onboarding or provider-backed agent command execution. Required pre-merge
candidate evidence remains a separate integration gap; do not infer PR readiness
from this release-workflow step. An optional `--root-proof-script` supplies an authenticated
Python validation client after install/reinstall. Its credential arrives only on stdin;
the runner verifies the resulting command-proof file against the actual service owner's
numeric identity and ownership. This hook is not itself evidence of a successful agent
run and remains pending executed validation.

### Codex handshake recovery

`provider/codex/client.go:sendRequest` / `send` retain two WebSocket attempts with the existing context-cancellable 300 ms backoff. `dialCodexWebsocket` marks rejected handshakes separately from established-socket error frames. `http_fallback.go:codexHandshakeShouldFallbackHTTP` admits only marked 5xx/426 responses after retry processing, with no earlier emitted event; authentication, rate-limit, cancellation, idle-timeout and partial-stream failures do not gain HTTP replay. `sendCodexResponsesHTTP` POSTs full input to the same OAuth endpoint with account/session headers and incremental SSE decoding, reporting `responses_http` rather than a successful WebSocket connection. No sticky fallback preference is stored: the next request starts with WebSockets again. Attack point: confusing a server error frame or prior output with a rejected handshake and duplicating generation. `http_fallback_test.go` authors local retry/order, OAuth routing, next-request preference, classifier and no-replay/cancellation assertions; all four focused fallback tests pass locally with Go 1.26.7, and repository pre-push plus critical-fast gates pass; live-provider evidence remains pending.

### Desktop development rebuild

`desktop-app-page.tsx` keeps the Rebuild Swarm control visible in expanded and focus sidebars whenever update status reports `dev_mode`, independently of release availability. It uses a rebuild icon without the release-availability badge. The existing local-administrative `/v1/update/run` route (`server_routes.go`, `handleUpdateRun`) still owns execution: `desktopUpdateKind` reads startup configuration, `configuredDevRoot` validates the configured source checkout, and the detached helper invokes `RunDevUpdate`. Explicit dev rebuilds always include Desktop assets, as `rebuild s` does, while retaining persisted job progress, failure reporting and service-manager restart. No shell command is accepted from the browser; release behavior and admission are unchanged. The launcher ordering regression asserts a full frontend build before install/reconcile/restart; preflight and reconciliation failure tests remain unexecuted evidence, not live proof.

### Required installed onboarding release dispatch

The canonical `scripts/run-testbench-launch-prerun.sh` default manifest includes
`installed-new-user`, `installed-existing-user`, and `installed-normal-user` in
addition to identity bootstrap. Each invokes an explicitly supplied reviewed
Python runner with structured `--archive`, `--checksum`, and `--case` arguments;
missing inputs fail, never skip. Guest-only selections do not load SSH environment
or preflight an unrelated pool. Attach-only refuses these mutating suites.
The existing supervisor retains the 600-second wall limit, bounded logs/heartbeat,
per-case outcomes and aggregate failure; installed cases receive 25 seconds of
cleanup grace. Recipe preparation is a separate prerequisite, not a second suite
manifest. Operator-owned runner locations and installation procedures stay outside
public source. Its contract checks real non-root process credentials, authenticated
resumability, optional-provider skip, explicit empty/selected-content Git consent,
retry uniqueness, access refusal/nonmutation and reinstall/reconnect preservation.
Runtime-user Git worktree writes are not provider-backed V3 allocation evidence.

Attack points: a bootstrap-only false pass, missing proof inputs, argv splitting,
rootful guests, process timeouts and swallowed child/cleanup failures.
`tests/scripts/installed_onboarding_dispatch_test.py` executes fake workers through
the actual manifest/supervisor and asserts exact argv, all outcomes, failure and
stall rejection. This is dispatch evidence, not installed-client proof. Existing
API regressions exercise `handleWorkspaceRepositoryBaseline`/`Review` and
`updateOnboarding` authentication, selected-content retries and resumability.
No API registration or product permission/ownership boundary changed. Independent
two-pass test review and fresh installed execution remain separate requirements;
no curated critical-test promotion is made.

### Native generation-wave membership

`run/allocateManagedDesignerArtifactV3` assigns a server-derived wave identity and contiguous managed-member order (excluding non-Designer launches), while retaining distinct initial-root allocation IDs and exact source-chain turns. `tool.ArtifactV3AuthorGrant.Generation` persists that immutable slot in the private grant. `pebble/prepareArtifactV3Generation`, inside `ApplySessionMutation`, adds public repository `generations` and an account/session/wave/index lookup in the same event/projection batch. Publication, failure, draft resume and recovery preserve existing memberships; grants without explicit membership stay ungrouped. No title, chronology or legacy-output rewrite/backfill is performed.

Existing §8 native `/v3/sessions/{session}/artifacts-v3` list returns local `generations`; detail adds bounded `generation_groups` (4096 total slots), each with exact artifact/turn/candidate/base identity, order/count, status and optional committed candidate OID. `runtime/generationGroups` and `SessionStore.GetArtifactV3Generation` authenticate the anchor and every indexed sibling against repository membership. Desktop consumers must retain this native metadata and use detail hydration for deep links; they must not infer groups or invoke selection on navigation. No new route or selection authority is introduced.

§10 invariant/attack points: occupied slots, changed grant identity, foreign owner/session, stale source base and corrupt index entries reject without changing the anchor/head. Membership is bounded to 256 per repository/wave. Allocation remains sequential: a preparation error can leave already allocated members, with declared count distinguishing an incomplete allocation; children are not started until the allocation pass returns. Tests prove failed *allocated* sibling retention, not an atomic all-wave reservation or provider launch crash recovery. Historical reconstruction is deliberately absent without a separately reviewed canonical-lineage migration.

Named Generation/HTTPGenerationContext/SceneLineage tests exercise allocation separation, immutable publication, failed draft status, actual Pebble close/reopen, source-chain reuse, foreign/corrupt/stale rejection and navigation nonmutation. Renderer in lineage tests is synthetic; no browser/provider/live proof or independent audit promotion is claimed.

- **2026-09-19 — Durable realtime usage accounting, snapshot-priced media accounting, and stored analytics:**
  - Separated conversational occupancy (`InputTokens`, `OutputTokens`, `RemainingTokens`) from cumulative billed consumption across turn and media pipelines; ensured updates and retries accumulate delta tokens/costs (`inputDelta`, `outputDelta`, `cachedDelta`, `thinkingDelta`, `deltaCost`, `deltaTokens`) instead of restating occupancy totals.
  - Snapshot pricing authority: eliminated all hardcoded fallback rates ($0.07 Veo, $0.04/$0.08 Lyria, $0.03 Imagen, assumed tokens) across video, audio, and image estimators. Unverified or missing catalog entries strictly report `PriceStatus: "unknown"` with `$0.00` cost.
  - Video estimator wires effective generation audio (`IncludesAudio: true` by default for Google Veo matching verified billing lines) and effective resolution conditions.
  - Image token extraction strictly inspects `candidatesTokensDetails` for modality `IMAGE`, never falling back to `totalTokenCount` (which includes input) or `candidatesTokenCount` (which includes text).
  - Propagated text pricing status (`CalculateCostWithStatus`) into `SessionTurnUsageSnapshot.PriceStatus`, surfacing unpriced models as `UnknownCount` in persisted `AccountUsageRollup` and dashboard summaries.
  - Unified persisted rollups under `AccountUsageRollup`, keyed by `account_usage_rollup/<accountScopeID>/<date>/<sessionID>/<provider>/<model>`, written atomically under combined session and `"account:" + accountScopeID` locks.
  - Replaced manual outbox/sequence paths with canonical `ApplyV3SessionMutation(V3SessionMutationRecordMediaUsage)`.
  - Analytics dashboard (`GET /v3/usage`) folds read-only directly from persisted rollups across date, session, provider, model, and archive filters with recent media list and propagated errors. Session rows and counts share that filtered population; missing period usage is not filled from lifetime summaries or session-library search. Summary/daily costs include media; Desktop treats the media field as a subset. Legacy lifetime-only data without rollups is not silently represented as period usage.
  - Desktop UI (`usage-page.tsx`) uses typed `DesktopV3CacheAction` invalidation on `reconnect.applySnapshot`, `realtime.applyEvent` (`run.usage.updated`, `session.media_usage.recorded`), and `syncStream.applyBatch`.

### Persistent automation session authority — 2026-09-11

`AutomationDefinition.session_id` pins an existing owned conversation in the reviewed policy digest. `PolicyApproval.ApproveUser` invokes the startup-wired `V3Runtime.acceptSession` only after explicit-user identity, current definition and plan checks. `session.automation.accepted` publishes a typed `SessionSnapshot.Automation` through `ApplyV3SessionMutation`; it does not replace messages or allocate a conversation. Approval grant creation and subsequent enable/link remain separate recoverable operations; a binding alone never authorizes execution.

`automation/persistent_session.go:ensurePersistent` uses that same owned repository lane, serializes occurrences regardless of legacy overlap setting, and installs `automation-plan:<execution-key>` plans without overwriting older plan results. `store/pebble/automation_session.go` checks current definition revision/policy and protects the binding from ordinary snapshot replacement. Reservation precedes plan installation; restart retries the same keys. User messages and unrelated run/plan admissions receive a conflict while an occurrence is reserved or active; the client must retain unsent text and retry after completion/cancellation. Blocked plans retain their reservation. Cancellation of an old occurrence cannot cancel another occurrence or user plan. Outcome reconciliation loads the occurrence-specific historical plan, not the latest active plan.

API catalog amendment (§8): existing `/v3/automations` definition writes now accept optional `session_id`; `/approve` performs in-place session acceptance when supplied. No new route. Tool conversational configuration and Desktop presentation are separate subsequent work. Existing definitions without the field retain the historical per-occurrence path; no transcript merge or automatic data migration occurs.

Critical area amendment (§10): likely attack points are forged session binding, stale definition acceptance, mutable policy overlay, user-turn/dispatch race, old-occurrence cancellation and historical outcome misattribution. Authorities are the explicit-user approval adapter, canonical session mutation lock, durable dispatch reservation and occurrence-keyed host fence. New `automation_session_test.go` authors preservation, foreign/stale rejection and missing-plan reservation cases; `persistent_session_test.go` authors immutable binding and digest cases. These are source-reviewed tests, not executed evidence. Full repeated-occurrence/restart and authenticated API integration proofs remain required; no curated test promotion.

Desktop `chat/services/slash-commands.ts` exposes `/integrate` in normal mode; `layout/desktop-app-page.tsx` captures the routed session, never the sidebar Git selection. `layout/integrate-command-dialog.tsx` presents source/target confirmation and explicit AI-commit-all consent, phase progress, preserved commit success, and a bounded error draft through `openIntegrationHelpDraft` and the existing session composer. It never auto-sends, archives, retries mutations, or rebuilds.

`git/integrate-command.ts` consumes the authenticated `/v3/sessions/{id}/repositories` inventory, selecting only the current session's unique active parent worktree across at most ten inventory pages. `attached`/`default` describe catalog attachment and are not managed-worktree eligibility gates. Selection shares `repositoryMutationSupported` with the Git sidebar for available, untruncated, exact-path Git evidence; incomplete/cyclic pagination and ambiguous parent rows fail before review or mutation. It uses existing commit suggestion/commit routes and `/v3/sessions:review-worktrees`; no route or Git executor was added. Confirmed target branch/HEAD and source lane are pinned across the commit. Post-commit inventory refresh supplies the exact new source HEAD. `promoteSessionsV3ReviewWorktrees` remains the authority for owned session, exact captured path/branch, clean source/target and HEAD checks, followed by `PrepareTaskIntegration` / `ApplyTaskIntegration`. Errors can follow Git success but metadata failure; UI explicitly requires inspection instead of claiming the target is unchanged. The existing commit authority is not a content-fingerprint transaction: concurrently edited source files can be included by its `git add --all` contract.

Critical-area / attack-point evidence: injected API tests in `git/integrate-command.spec.ts` exercise normal-mode availability, clean and dirty ordering, missing/foreign/ambiguous lanes, dirty/stale target rejection before mutation, AI/commit failures, partial success, bounded repair text and single-flight rejection. The slash palette also stops suggesting partial command matches once unmatched arguments are entered, satisfying its existing retired-command regression. These are workflow tests, not proof of live Git atomicity or browser interaction. No curated runner promotion; independent test review and live UI inspection remain outstanding.
